CXVIII). » De hujus arboris fructu, quasi languidus in ultima desperationis infirmitate, mali granati succo in adventu ejus recreata est credentium turba, qui fructus quotidie per eos qui Christi vices agunt, in guttur Ecclesiae infundi monstratur, et sic per singulos gradus profectuum introducitur ad arcanam intelligentiam legis divinae, ut agnoscere possit omnia illa quae in Veteri Testamento sunt gesta. Ait enim: « Sicut malum inter ligna silvarum, sic dilectus meus inter filios. » Ac si dicatur: Sicut malum visu, odore et gustu antecedit omnia ligna silvestria, ita Christus antecedit omnes sanctos qui filii Dei vocantur. De quibus dicitur: 589B« Tunc exsultabunt omnia ligna silvarum ante faciem Domini quoniam venit (Psal. XCV). » Unde filii non malitia vocantur, sicut filiae, quae spinis comparantur. Est quippe magna exsultatio lignis silvarum, dum conspiciunt in medio sui talem arborem surrexisse, cujus fructus totam decoravit silvam, omnes abstulit animarum languores, omnesque indulcoravit fructus amaritudinis. Sub cujus umbra omni aestu tristitiae, omnique pondere peccati abjecto, Ecclesia multo tempore desideratam requiem inventam, et dulcissimo fructu saginata adolescentibus animabus adepta gaudia narrat. « Sub umbra quam desiderabam, inquit, sedi, et fructus ejus dulcis gutturi meo. » Ac si dicat: Eodem protegente quem semper adesse quaesivi quieta ac secura 589Cmaneo, et gratia sua coelestis dulcedinis me fecit esse participem: et sic per ordinem ad altiora intelligenda mysteria se introduci testatur dicendo:

« Introduxit me rex in cellam vinariam, ordinavit in me charitatem. » Cella vinaria Ecclesia debet intelligi, sicut nonnulli opinantur, in cujus unitate Spiritus sanctus tantummodo dari solet et accipi. Cujus gratia hoc loco vini nomine designatur. Vinum, cum Spiritum sanctum accepissent apostoli, Judaei dixerunt: « Musto pleni sunt isti (Act. II). » In qua cella ordinata est charitas, ut quisque Deum toto corde plusquam seipsum, et proximum tanquam seipsum diligat. Quanquam possit ex superfluo cella vinaria mysterium passionis Christi intelligi, in 589Dquo introducta est Ecclesia, quando per fidem ejus sacramentis participare meruit. Sed quia per ordinem adolescentibus animabus intelligenda mysteria introductam se testatur, necessario cellam vinariam, scientiam et dulcedinem divinae legis vult intelligi. Ad quam dulcedinem invitat adolescentulas, et laudando festinare hortatur, quam Christus propinando docuit, cum ait: « Operamini cibum qui non perit, sed qui permanet in aeternum (Joan VI). » In quo opere positos beatificat Propheta dicens: « Beati qui scrutantur testimonia ejus, in toto corde exquirunt eum (Psal. CXVIII). » Sunt enim testimonia in Veteri Testamento legis, quae Christum humani generis Redemptorem probant venturum, advenisse, 590Aiterum adventurum in gloriam. Ponamus igitur legem latissimum esse palatium, in quo rex inhabitat Christus, sermo omnipotentis Patris, ubi ad aeternam militiam introducuntur credentium animae, ubi introductam se, in eamdem videlicet intelligentiam legis dicit ad consideranda mirabilia quae per singulos patriarchas vel prophetas usque ad partum Virginis operatus est Deus, quam nunc per figuram cellam vinariam nominavit, ubi introducta, suscepit in se ordinem charitatis. In quo ordine charitatis, quid aliud credendum est primum imbui, nisi ut credat et agnoscat primum debere panem nominari, in quo Filius semper, ut Verbum invocet; secundum Filium in quo semper Pater, tertium Spitum sanctum, qui vera ratione de voce et verbo de 590BPatre et Filio procedere comprobatur? qui per omnia Deus unus diligendus est, secundum illud in initio Decalogi: « Diliges Dominum Deum tuum in toto corde tuo. » Secundus ordo est, « in tota anima tua. » Tertius ordo est, « in tota virtute tua (Exod. XX). » Deinde jam in singulis praeceptis legalibus quomodo, vel quo affectu debeat diligi proximus, vel singulae personae in Ecclesia, charitas ordinat. Alio enim ordine reciproca charitas pensanda est Deo, qui nos secundum apostolum (I Joan. IV) prior dilexit, qui nobis omnem summam charitatis per Christum contulisse probatur. Alio ordine fideli amico, alio ordine charissimo filio, alio ordine impendenda genitoribus, per quos sumus qui non eramus, alio ordine fratri germano, alio ordine conjugi; alio ordine servus 590Cdomino, alio ordine dominus servo, alio peregrino, alio agnito, alio sacerdoti, alio propinquis et proximis. Nam largiri necessaria egenti infirmo, vel peregrino, non ad ostensionem laudis humanae, sed propter retributionem aeternam ordinatae charitatis indicium est. Consolari indigentes, et oppressos injusto judicio, visitare infirmos vel carceri mancipatos, ordinata charitas demonstratur. In nullo negotio pro veritate hominis personam revereri, misericorditer admonere insipientes, veracissime increpare superbos, charitatis manifestus est ordo. Quisquis autem amplius diligit patrem, aut matrem, uxorem, filios, vel fratres, aut divitias praesentis temporis quam Deum, est quidem in eo charitas, sed non ordinata, quoniam prae omnibus ipsum 590Ddiligere debemus, et proximum sicut nosmetipsos. Iste est ordo charitatis. Ordinavit ergo Dei Filius in Ecclesia sua charitatem, ut sciat unusquisque Ecclesiae filius unicuique quo ordine charitatis impendat officium, dicendo in Evangelio: « Reddite quae sunt Caesaris Caesari, et quae sunt Dei Deo (Matth. XXII). » Et per Apostolum ait: « Reddite singulis debita, cui honorem, honorem: cui timorem, timorem: cui tributum, tributum: cui vectigal, vectigal (Rom. XII). » Non omnes qui Christiano vocabulo nuncupantur, per hunc ordinem charitatis in praedictam intelligentiam, quam cellam vinariam nominavit, introducuntur, sed qui parvum quantumcunque imitatus fuerit Paulum, qui dicebat (II Cor. XI): Amplius om 591Anibus laboravi, in vigiliis, in jejuniis, et caetera: « et omnia, ait, arbitratus sum ut stercora, ut Christum lucrifacerem (Phil. III). » In capite hujus cantici in cubiculum, sive cellarium regis introducta Ecclesia laudat, ubi ingressa dicit, « Exsultabimus et laetabimur in te, » agnitam videlicet ejus praesentiam corporalem, qui est sapientia Patris, qui velut in conclavi prophetarum praeconiis latebat, in quo sunt omnes exsultationes, et laetitiae, et thesauri. Hic vero ad intelligendam divinam legem, et ad ordinem charitatis discendum se introductam dicit, non se laetari et exsultare, sed languere, eo quod, secundum Salomonem (Eccle. I): « Qui addit scientiam, addit dolorem. » Et quis quantum consectando proximius effici coeperit sapientiae, tantum eam vix conspicit prolongatam. 591BDe quo dolore beatus amor nascitur Christi, et de amore gloriosior sanitas, animae languor, qui ei omnes vires subtrahat ad peccandum. Quae licet arcana mysteria multiformis sapientiae Dei adepta sit, quae licet perfectionis teneat arcem, non sibi tamen solius praedictae sententiae aut intelligentiae credit sufficere summam, nisi stipata mali et fulcita diversis fuerit floribus. Unde continuo subjunxit:

« Fulcite me floribus, stipate me malis, quia amore langueo. » Praesentiam corporalem sapientiae Dei Patris, id est Christum, requirit in cella scientiae legis, sed quia illum quem desiderat videre prout est adhuc non permittitur, aliorum exempla ad suam consolationem exposcit, quibus interim suum languorem temperare valeat. Ait namque: 591C« Fulcite me floribus, stipate me malis, » id est consolamini me exemplis seu inchoantium bonum opus, seu perficientium: quia adhuc in peregrinationis taedio, amore supernae visionis languesco. Unde non immerito per flores animae jam bonum opus inchoantes, et coelesti desiderio redolentes, per mala de floribus perfectae jam bonorum mentes, quae ad fructum perveniunt boni operis, de initio sanctae propositionis intelliguntur. Quae ergo amore languet, fulciri quaerit floribus, stipari malis, quia nullum quem desiderat videre adhuc non permittitur, magna est consolatio si de aliorum profectibus laetetur, ut anima requiescat in bono opere proximi, quae adhuc contemplari non valet vultum Dei. Videamus qualis mens Pauli fuerat, qui dicebat: « Mihi vivere 591DChristus est et mori lucrum (Phil. I). » Hinc est quod iterum dicit: « Desiderium habeo dissolvi et cum Christo esse multo magis melius (Ibid.). » Sed ecce qui dissolvi desideras, quo amore langueas videamus. Quia interim differri te conspicis fulciri, quaeso, floribus non requiris? Quaeris plane: nam sequitur: « Permanere autem in carne, necessarium existimo propter vos (Ibid.). » Et proficientibus discipulis dicit: « Quae est enim nostra spes aut gaudium, aut corona gloriae? Nonne vos ante Dominum nostrum Jesum Christum? » (I Thes. II.) Conjuncta ergo anima Dei verbo, introducta intelligentiam legis, discurrendo per singulos apices Scripturae, ut saepe dictum est, et singula vasa cellae vinariae 592Adivinorum librorum degustando sapores, quasi inebriata laetitiae vino semper futurae beatitudinis, quamvis sponsum non cernat in praesenti, cur ad eam tarde perveniat dicit se amore languere. Illo procul dubio, de quo Propheta dicit: « Sitivit anima mea ad Deum fontem vivum, quando veniam et apparebo ante faciem Domini? » (Psal. XLI.) Per quem amorem scientiae, amor germinatur vitae aeternae, et de amore vitae aeternae tolerantia persecutionis. In quo amore saporata, quasi in lectulo collocata, promissionis laeva et adjutorii dextera Christi amplexari se deprecabatur dicendo:

« Laeva ejus sub capite meo, et dextera illius amplexabitur me. » Quid namque per sinistram nisi vita praesens, vel temporalia Dei omnipotentis dona 592Bdesignantur? Quid vero per dexteram, nisi perpetua vita figuratur? Sinistra namque Dei, Ecclesia videlicet, prosperitatem vitae praesentis et temporalia dona quasi sub capite posuit, quam intentione summi amoris premit. Dextera vero Dei eam amplectitur quia sub aeterna ejus beatitudine tota devotione continetur. Quoniam hic per spem sponsus mentem roborat, et illic per remunerationem glorificat. Vel in laeva Vetus Testamentum, et in dextera Novum Testamentum intimatur. Nam potest ita intelligi, ut laeva Christi a sinistris sustineat animam, ne ab incentivis vitiorum uncinis implanetur in laeva, sed semper ostentata occultis compunctionibus sublimetur ad regnum. Et ne in superbiam elata faciat lapsum in dextera in qua diabolus corruit, tribulationes 592Cet multimoda retinacula permittendo, et fatigata adjuvando dextera sua amplexatur. Valet etiam ex superfluo ita intelligi, ut laeva sub capite fidei sit scutum, quod pugnans sinistra contineat manu, et dexterae amplexatio orationis gladius intelligatur, quo dextera semper armatus, quatenus altero armorum genere adversarius repellatur, et altero prosternatur. Sequitur:

« Adjuro vos, filiae Hierusalem, per capreas cervosque camporum, ne suscitetis neque evigilare faciatis dilectam, quoadusque ipsa velit. » Post multos labores, scilicet Ecclesiae, et discursus, per quos anxia intra sanctam cellam vinariam fatigata redditur in soporem, quae celerius ne pristinis reddatur laboribus suscitata a somno, adjurantur filiae 592DHierusalem ne suscitent eam; et per hoc adjurantur quod firma diligatur charitate. Per capreas videlicet cervosque camporum, quas non parvi meriti vel laboris animas esse intelligi datur, per quas adjurantur filiae Hierusalem. In plebe enim Christiana secundum meritorum qualitatem diversis animalibus animae comparantur. Habet ergo palatium Dominus noster Jesus Christus, quod intra se cellaria multa, et cellam vinariam, et camporum spatia continere intelligitur. Cujus flores beatus Paulus ingressus, admirando laudat: « O altitudo, inquit, divitiarum sapientiae et scientiae Dei! (Rom. XI.) » In quo palatio inter multas divitias, id est multitudinem sanctorum, intelligitur habere etiam animas diversas 593Asicut in domo magna, juxta Apostolum (II Thes. II), non solum sunt vasa aurea et argentea, sed et fictilia et lignea. Unde non immerito filiae Hierusalem animae sunt electorum supernae patriae visionem pacis contemplantes. Capreas vero vel cervos spirituales Veteris Novique Testamenti panes appellat, quibus multitudo sacrarum Scripturarum pascua est. Seu capreas eas nonnulli animas intelligi volunt, quae acumine dialecticae, intelligentiae sensu per philosophiam et mente discurrere consuerunt, quae nunc intra supradictum palatium rete conclusae arte venatorum, id est apostolorum, caeterorumque doctorum manu captae, mutata religione in melius, campos divinarum Scripturarum, vel per sapientiae quaeque ardua et obscura interpretantes, discurrunt 593Bmontes, et cultura deorum in Creatoris laudem quotidie vertitur. Quaeque in fide velocitatem sui cursus ostendunt, et acumine sensus more caprearum insidias adversariorum a longe prospiciunt. Caprearum natura est primam corporis partem sublimiorem, humiliorem posteriorem probatam habere. Et hac de causa semper sursum ascendendo montem in cursu velocior invenitur, quo itinere adversarius minor est insequendo. Cervi autem videntur intelligi, qui in ipsa philosophiae suae velocitate doctrinae non multos, sed unum Deum invisibilem, immensum, inaestimabilem, omniumque Creatorem, totum ubique mundum implentem confessi sunt, qui et ipsi a sanctis doctoribus rete praedicationis in sinum Ecclesiae capti sunt. Qui in arduis vitae morantur, 593Cmore cervorum fontem aquarum petunt, serpentes interimunt, et tanquam munda animalia ruminant. Hi sunt ergo procul dubio cervi camporum et capreae, animae videlicet fidelium philosophorum conversae ab infidelitate superstitionum, per quos sponsus filias Hierusalem conjurat, ut patiantur paulisper requiescere sponsam in requiei somno, quae delectantur Ecclesiam secum concurrere in illis operibus in quibus ipsae ad coelestem sunt gloriam sublimatae, per quae, amissa corporea sarcina, cum illis epulentur in coelis. Quam Dominus Christus ad lucrum vel consolationem aliarum animarum requiescere in corpore jubet quoadusque ipsa velit, donec ipse sponsus prospiciens utilitati ejus et ipse cognoscat vires suas: quia sine ipso nihil potest facere. 593DSed potest juxta intelligentiam priorem, ubi capreas et cervos Veteris et Novi Testamenti Patres significare diximus, ita intelligi: Contestor pacificas, ac si dicatur fidelium animas, per suas quasque virtutes, quae per munda et ruminantia animalia significatae sunt, ne pia fratrum studia aliqua importunitate impediant, sed sic quisque de proximorum profectu, quasi de suo gaudeat. Aliter admonet Christus ne quisquam doctorum muneribus scilicet vel minis deterreat credituros, sed tantum quae sancta sunt praedicent, et voluntarie ad fidem vel ad religionem credituros vel conversos adducant, et in sua tranquillitate non perturbent, donec divina inspiratione adacti ad altioris vitae studia accingantur. Sed perfecta 594Aelectorum anima, et, ut ita dicam, sancta Ecclesia in ipso requiei sopore semper eum timendo audit, semper eum amando videt. Unde nunc ait, subauditur, haec est quam audivi adjurantem filias Hierusalem, ne me in ejus amplexu quiescentem suscitarent. Vel vox illius evangelica est praedicatio. Et quid dicat vox ista dilecti Ecclesiae, in ipso Evangelio proprio ore declarat. « Videte, inquit, ne seducamini a pseudoprophetis. Multi enim venient in nomine meo dicentes, quia ego sum Christus, et multos seducent, et dabunt signa, ita ut etiam, si fieri potest, electi in errorem inducantur (Matth. XXIV). » Et: « Videte ne graventur corda vestra crapula aut ebrietate (Luc. XXI). » Et: « Similes estote servis exspectantibus dominum suum, quando 594Brevertatur a nuptiis (Luc. XII). » Ne sub specie doctorum decipiatur Ecclesia requiescens in amplexibus sponsi. Et ne otio post labores torpescat, aut deliciis resolvatur, in Evangelio taliter admonetur. Sed in ipsa securitatis requie sollicita semper aurem cordis ad vocem Christi praebere, hoc est in Scripturis divinis, et assiduae orationi operam dare studeat. Monentur ergo per haec qui praesunt populo Christiano ut sine intermissione annuntient plebi quid praecipiat vox ista dilecti Ecclesiae, quomodo scilicet venit de sinu Patris ad humanum consortium ut Verbum fieret caro, quomodo secundum Habacuc prophetam (Cap. III) humiliando daemonum superbiam, confregerit montes violentiae, et liquefecerit colles aeternales, conculcando sub pedibus credentium 594CSatanam, transiliens montes praedictos. Et hoc perpendens Ecclesia ait:

« Ecce venit saliens in montibus, transiliens colles. » Vis audire quales saltus fecit? Tales, inquam, saltus dilectus meus. De coelo venit in uterum, de utero in praesepe, de praesepe venit in crucem, de cruce venit in sepulcrum, de sepulcro rediit in coelum: ipse est qui elevatus est etiam super omnes montes et colles, id est Patres Novi et Veteris Testamenti, qui merito virtutum et vertice fidei caeteris sunt eminentiores. In his Dominus non solum inhabitare, sed transilire dicitur, quia non tantum divinitate, sed humanitate incomparabilis apparuit. Seu per apostolos, ut dicere coepimus superius, omnem superbiam sapientiae mundi extollentem se 594Dadversus Creatorem liquefecit, et ad nihilum redacta humiliatur. In quibus non rhetorica argumenta, sed sancti Spiritus virtus fulgebat, et prostrata daemonum turba sub pedibus credentium, ostensus est Ecclesiae saliens in montibus, et transiliens colles venisse in mundum, et inter homines conversatus est. Unde et mox subjungitur:

« Similis est dilectus meus capreae hinnuloque cervorum. » Capreae Christum comparatum non per detrimenta divinitatis qui semper idem est, intelligimus, sed per incarnationis mysterium, videlicet quia apparuit in similitudinem carnis peccati in quo augmenta consecuta est, coelo recepta carnis natura. Nam et capreae ideo nonnulli assimilatum opinantur, 595Aquia nostrae salutis illustratio caecitatem cordis auferens in posteriore saeculi parte veniendo in carne se ostendit, quam Moysi petenti in monte Sina monstraverat. De quo mysterio David dicit: « Pennae columbae deargentatae in posteriora dorsi ejus (Psal. LXVII). » Sicut caprearum posterior corporis pars candorem demonstrat; et quia multi non solum ex Judaeis, sed etiam ex philosophis gentium credidere per ejus humilitatem incarnationis, de quibus intelliguntur agrestes illae animae quondam mundiores caeteris supradictis, quae per ipsius doctrinam ad unius veri Israelis consortium aggregatae sunt. Talibus similis juxta opinionem aliquorum voce Ecclesiae pronuntiatur, qui propter innocentum et multitudinem credentium hinnulo cervorum comparatur. 595BHinnulus cervorum ideo dicitur, quia genitus est ex stirpe patriarcharum, qui cervorum nomine praenotantur, seu hinnulo cervorum propter humilitatis formam, necdum adhuc potentiae cornibus exaltatis, quibus in secundo adventu causaturus est mundum, de quibus dictum est in psalmo: Tunc « exsultabuntur cornua justi (Psal. LXXIV). » Hinnulus enim cervorum sine cornibus est, amorem in se potius visionis suae quam terribilitatem ostendit, sicut Dominus noster Christus in primo adventu fecisse dignoscitur. De cujus primo adventu mox subjungitur. « En ipse stat post parietem nostrum, respiciens per fenestras, prospiciens per cancellos. » Indutus pariete nostrae mortalitatis, latuit substantia divinitatis. Sed prospiciens ad nos 595Cper cancellos et fenestras dum miracula fecit, ut ex miraculis appareret qui ex passionibus latuit. Qui enim humanis oculis hoc quod de mortali natura assumpsit ostendit, et in se ipso, qui invisibilis permansit, in aperto se videre quaerentibus quasi post parietem stetit, quia videndum se manifestata majestate non praebuit. Quasi enim post parietem stetit, qui humanitatis naturam quam assumpsit ostendit, et divinitatis naturam humanis oculis occultavit. Quisquis enim per fenestras vel cancellos respicit, nec totus videtur, nec totus non videtur. Redemptor enim noster, quia et divina fecit et humana pertulit, quasi per cancellos et fenestras ad homines venit ut Deus, ut appareret ex miraculis, sicut dictum est, et lateret ex passionibus, et homo cerneretur ex passionibus. 595DRecte dicitur: « En ipse stat post parietem nostrum, » et reliqua. Potest, si cui libuerit, ita intelligi: En ipse qui crucifixus est, qui divinitatis suae dextera languentem, id est lugentem in apostolis consolationis amplexu continebat Ecclesiam, cum qua carne sepultus est, cum ipsa utique stat resurgens post parietem incredulitatis nostrae quem luteis operibus construxeramus peccando. De quibus dicit per Isaiam prophetam: « Nunquid non valet manus mea ad liberandum? Sed peccata vestra murum fecerunt inter vos et Deum (Isa. L). » Post quem stans Dominus Christus exspectat se ab impiis invocari, et vocat ad poenitentiam animam peccatricem, exspectat sibi ab incredulis credi. Post quem parietem 596Astat, usque ad tempus baptismatis vel poenitentiae. Et licet non mereamur eum intueri, tamen per hoc quod idololatriam abdicamus, fenestras in supradicto facimus pariete, quibus nos ab immundis spiritibus defendendo respicit Christus. Et ipsi soli genua cordis deflectendo cancellos facimus, per quos compunctionem ad se convertendo prospiciat. Ubi vero venerimus ad veram conversionem baptismi praedicti vel poenitentiae, retollit eum de medio concessa venia peccatorum, et loquitur nobis dicendo: « Venite ad me, omnes qui laboratis et onerati estis, et ego reficiam vos (Matth. XI). » Et: « Venite, benedicti Patris mei, possidete paratum vobis regnum a constitutione mundi (Matth. XXV). » Sicut et magister gentium, Paulus, docuit factum in ejus adventu. 596B« Qui tulit, inquit, parietem maceriae de medio, et reconciliavit nos Deo Patri per sanguinem ipsius (Ephes. II). » Unde cum inclusa esset Ecclesia quodammodo intra parietum septa propter persecutionis metum, post suam resurrectionem formidantibus et nimio terrore dejectis Apostolis, januis clausis ingressus, magnam fiduciam magnumque gaudium apportavit dicendo: « Pax vobis. Ego sum, nolite timere (Luc. XXIV). » Tunc completum est quod sequitur:

« Dilectus meus loquitur mihi: Surge, propera, amica mea, columba mea, formosa mea, et veni. » Loquitur ei scilicet ad perfectionem vitae vocando, dicendo ei jam non timere mortis auctorem, diabolum, nec ultra residere intra incredulitatis et torporis 596Cclaustra. « Propera, » inquit, « et veni, » quia ego moriendo vitam perditam reddidi. Modo jam non terrearis a morte, cui ego destruxi imperium. Stabat namque post parietem nostrum, quia nobis umbra sua obstabat sol justitiae, de quo propheta promiserat dicens: « Orietur vobis timentibus Deum sol justitiae, et sanitas in pennis ejus (Mal. IV). » Qui loquitur Ecclesiae in apostolis, quando surgens a mortuis consolatur eos, dicens: « Confidite, quia ego vici mundum (Joan. XVI). » Et: « Data est mihi omnis potestas in coelo et in terra (Matth. XXVIII). » Et: « Nolite timere eos qui occidunt corpus, animam autem non possunt occidere (Matth. XI). » Unde per hanc sententiam ad praedicationis officium provocatur, sicut ipsa dicit: « dilectus meus loquitur 596Dmihi, » id est ad praedicandum me hortatur dicens: « Surge, propera, amica mea, et veni, » id est surge de stratu quietudinis, in quo solius tui curam agere quaeris, propera, et veni, ad implendam etiam proximi curam salutis, studium sedulae praedicationis officio impende. Nam et verbis affabilibus tribuit fiduciam dum subjungit:

« Jam enim hiems transiit, imber abiit et recessit. Flores apparuerunt in terra nostra, tempus putationis advenit. » Docet eam scilicet jam non timere omnino iniquorum hominum truculentiam, nec daemonum formidare terrores, quia et Deum Patrem per assumptae carnis oblationem reconciliavit Ecclesiae, et daemonum exercitum subnervavit per crucem, 597Aet post brumalem gelidamque asperitatem floridum verni adventus sui tempus ostendit, et quanta vel qualia gaudiorum esset praemia ei per suam praesentiam exhibiturus, futuram jam quasi factam narravit. Unde et ei dicitur: « Exsurge, propera, amica mea, formosa mea, et veni. » Amica enim fit agnoscendo Deum; formosa, Christi humilitatem servando. Columba vero efficitur, nihil de terrenis cupiditatibus absque cibo vilissimo, indumentoque corporis requirendo, sed semper columbarum simplicitatem tenens, Spiritui sancto se sociat. Vel amica dicitur per dilectionem, formosa vel speciosa per decorem virtutum et lavacrum regenerationis, columba per infusionem Spiritus sancti. Sicut igitur superveniente verno praecursore aestatis qui fugat hiemem, 597Bquando omnis creatura rediviva laetatur, et universa animalia secundum genus suum corporantur ad fetus, et ingravidata cubilia construunt, et aves nidos componunt, et sese ad invicem suis vocibus discretis advocant et motibus, ubi jam reptilibus epulas praeparat humus ne deesset pennigeris animantibus esca, ubi canora vox volatilium dulcis modulamine resonat et pipat, et ad praedandos flores apis pennis armata procedit: ita Dominus noster Jesus Christus, post horridam hiemem idololatriae et philosophicam doctrinam, vernum tempus per suam passionem, quod est pascha nostrum, transitus de morte ad vitam, faciem mundi martyrum, vel omnium sanctorum operum, et initia fidei credentium flore decoravit. Eo tempore de quietis stratu ad officium 597Cpraedicationis, imo de convalle lacrymarum ad paradisi Dei montem suae mortis exemplo Ecclesiam vocat. Denique cum dixisset, « Surge, propera, amica mea, formosa mea, et veni, » mox subjunxit, « jam enim hiems transiit, » id est potestas tenebrarum, quae tristem reddebat mundum, sole justitiae Christo veniente, transit de hoc mundo ad tartara. Jam frigus infidelitatiis et imber iniquitatis recesserunt, quae totum orbem usque ad tempus Dominicae incarnationis et resurrectionis tegebant. « Flores apparuerunt in terra nostra, » id est initia fidei et justitiae floruerunt in mundo crescente Ecclesia. « Tempus putationis advenit, » id est amputatis inutilibus vanae religionis, sarmentis futurae fidei praeparantur corda hominum, aestivum namque tempus advenit, 597Dquando ex una stirpe corporis boni separantur a malis, ne vicinitate malorum depereant boni, ne perfidia incredulorum periclitentur credentes. Hoc utique tempus nuntiabatur venire putationis, de quo Dominus dicit in Evangelio: « Veni enim separare filium a patre, et nurum a socru sua, et filiam a matre sua (Matth. X), » et reliqua. Vel hiems transiit, quia praesentis vitae tribulatio abscessit. Imber quoque abiit, quia cum ad contemplandum in sua substantia omnipotentem Deum ducimur, jam verborum guttae necessariae non erunt, ut pluvia debeat praedicationis infundi. Nam quod minus hic audire quis potest, ibi amplius videbit: « Quia nunc videmus per speculum in aenigmate, tunc autem facie ad faciem, 598Aet videbimus eum sicuti est (I Cor. XIII). » Tunc apparent flores in terra, quia cum de aeternae beatitudinis vita quaedam suavitatis primordia praegustare anima coeperit, quasi jam in floribus odoratur exiens, quod postquam egressa fuerit in fructu uberius habebit, unde et illuc subditur: « Tempus putationis advenit. » In putatione quippe sarmenta sterilia reciduntur, ut ea quae praevalent uberius fructum ferant. Nostrae itaque putationis tempus tunc adveniet, quando infructuosam ac noxiam corruptionis carnem deserimus. Qui fructus nobis erit uberrimus, visio unius Dei. Ut tempus putationis est, vocatio e mundo animae coelestium. Sequitur: « Vox turturis audita est in terra nostra. » Dicamus opiniones diversorum, ne forte ignorasse causari videamur. Vocem 598Bturturis propter decorem castitatis beatam Mariam intelligi volunt, cujus vox primum audita est in terra nostra dicendo ad angelum: « Quomodo fiet istud, quia virum non cognosco? » (Luc. I.) Et: « Ecce ancilla Domini, fiat mihi secundum verbum tuum (Ibid.) » . Et: « Ex hoc beatam me dicent omnes generationes (Ibid.) » . Cui angelus respondit: « Spiritus sanctus superveniet in te, et virtus Altissimi obumbrabit tibi. » Et: « Quod nascetur ex te sanctum vocabitur Filius Dei. » Et vere dignam, quam solam beatam omnes generationes dicunt, quam gloriosam inter omnes feminas non solum diversarum gentium nationes, sed etiam coelorum virtutes admirantes collaudant, per vitae ingressum enim, mortem fugatam, mundum reconciliatum gaudemus. Aliqui vero beatum Joannem 598CBaptistam asserunt, cujus vox audita est dicentis: « Ego vox clamantis in deserto, parate viam Domini (Matth. V). » Et: « Facite fructus dignos poenitentiae (Luc. III). » Quandiu enim praevaricationis hiems tempus triste, constrictumque ab omni germine bono, obtexerat faciem mundi, et sua ditione obtinebat diabolus terram, haec dulcissima vox turturis, sive per beatam Mariam, sive per Joannem non est audita in terra. Sed ubi magnus ille sol justitiae, qui nobis indulgentia aestatis ubertatem adducendo exortus est, coepit desiderio conservandae castitatis vox turturis in terra nostra audiri. Justa enim ratione turturi castissimae avi virginitas comparatur, quae in Joanne vel Maria obtinet principatum. 598DQuae semel Verbo Dei vel Spiritui sancto conjuncta, nunquam alium cogitat comparem, nec altera desideriorum sui amoris conjunctio est, nisi illi cui semel conglutinata probatur, tanquam turtur qui deserti dilecti habitacula diligit, et advocans comparem dulci resonat voce, et eo amisso alium non requirit, sed ad alium semper promissionis vocem et mentis emittit, cui promisit servare dilectionis affectum. Quo mortuo, nunquam alio conjungi narratur; sed eum omnibus diebus vitae, cui semel fuerit conjuncta, desiderando requirit. Sed sciendum est, licet ita sit vox turturis quae audita est in terra nostra: illa est Domini Salvatoris, quae dicit in Evangelio: « Poenitentiam agite, appropinquabit enim regnum coelorum (Matth. III). » Vel per turturem rectissime 599Asancta Ecclesia intelligitur, varietate virtutum velut plumis ornata.

« Ficus protulit grossos suos. » Propinquante igitur messe redemptionis humanae et gloriosi Redemptoris adventu, impietatis hieme et algore constricta nihil in se ficulnea, id est Synagoga, nisi amarissimum succum doctrinae quem animarum cibum mentitur, intelligitur habuisse. Sed in Domini adventu postquam turturis vox audita est dicentis, « Poenitentiam agite, » et reliqua, ficus protulit grossos suos, id est praecepta legis ceciderunt et ipsa per apostolos protulit grossos suos, qui de ea generati dulcissimus cibus doctrinae animarum effecti sunt. Sed quia eis credere noluit, aeterna sterilitate aruit, et omni decore nudata nihil in se nisi ignis aeterni 599Balimentum conservata est. A cujus perversae oppressionis umbra liberata plebs, coepit, amotis malis doctoribus Pharisaeis, in Christo credendo florere, et suo exemplo aliarum gentium nationes ad florem credulitatis provocare. De quibus sequenti versiculo dicitur: « Vineae florentes dederunt odorem suum. » Credendo namque in unum Deum omnipotentem floruerunt vineae, id est evangelicus populus. Seu vineae florentes, sanctae conversationes odorem longe lateque dederunt per orbem. Nam sicut hiemis tempore absque frondibus et floribus sine decore sunt vineae, ita fuisse absque decore justitiae et sanctitatis ornamento per orbem nationes quas nunc vineas appellavit, certissime comprobatur. Sequitur:

« Surge, propera, amica mea, speciosa mea, et 599Cveni, columba mea, in foraminibus petrae, in caverna maceriae. » Jacebat ergo satis in humili loco et abdito Ecclesia multum a longe vocata, quae nunc in toto mundo regnare probatur, conturbata vel oppressa de daemonum tempestate. Sed ubi passiones Christi sacramenta sunt celebrata per apostolorum doctrinam, in quibus Christus loquitur, advocatur ut surgat et veniat ad deambulantem sponsum in vineis ad congaudendum, et in proventum fructus justitiae praedictarum nationum se exerat. Ac si ei dicatur: O sponsa et amica, cui tanta obtuli bona et pro qua tanta sustinui, surge et veni, accingere ad certamen, unde aeternam quietem accipias « in foraminibus petrae, in caverna maceriae. » Quid sit petra Apostolus exponit: « Petra, inquit, erat Christus (I Cor. 599DX). » Quicunque ergo desiderat ab hostibus aeriae potestatis eripi, et illaesus evadere, in foraminibus hujus petrae, fidem servando semper ingrediatur, id est consideret vulnera clavorum et lanceae quae pro salute sponsae sponsus suscepit, et in caverna maceriae, hoc est custodia virtutum coelestium. Habet enim ista petra multa foramina, id est aditus, per quos intratur ad Patrem regnum credentibus praeparantem, hoc est mansuetudinis, humilitatis, patientiae, contemptus divitiarum, nullarum acceptio personarum, pernoctatio in oratione, misericordiae magisterium, castitas immobilis, forma benignitatis, singulare speculum, individuae Trinitatis agnitio, in qua est nostra redemptio. Haec sunt procul dubio 600Aforamina in quibus docetur anima ad gaudia regni coelorum venire. Potest foramen petrae intelligi, protectio ejusdem Domini Jesu Christi, ad quam confugit Ecclesia, velut columba ad foramen petrae. Hinc Isaias ait: « Ingredere in petram, abscondere in fossa humo (Isa. II). » Caverna maceriae Spiritus sancti defensio, vel congregatio fidelium designari potest, quanquam foramina petrae quatuor Evangeliorum assertiones valeant intelligi, quae ejusdem petrae perforatum supradictis ictibus corpus testantur. Caverna vero maceriae apostolorum doctrinae, de quibus ut vineae credentium plebi firmissimam maceriam ob infestationem malignorum spirituum Deum construxisse probatur. Nam sicut ipsi per Christum, ita et nos per apostolos agnoscentes divina 600Bmysteria, intra conspectum fidei quam ipsi confessi sunt continemur. Et quia « fides sine operibus otiosa est (Jac. II), » recte subjungitur. « Ostende mihi faciem tuam. Sonet vox tua in auribus meis. Vox enim tua dulcis, et facies tua decora. » Ac si aperte dicatur: Ostende mihi faciem tuam, precor, in publicum actionis procede, noli sub quietis torpore quiescere, et operibus ostende fidem tuam. Et sonet vox tua, id est praedicationis, laudis et confessionis in auribus meis. Illius namque vox dulcis est Deo, qui dulce et suaviter ex dulcedine regni verba Domini proximis enuntiare, et ipsi Domino laudes resonare novit. Et illa fides decora est, quae, operibus ornata, adversa pati non timet. Sequitur:

« Capite nobis vulpes parvulas quae demoliuntur 600Cvineas; nam vinea nostra floruit. » Transacta videlicet daemonum tempestate, quam superius hiemem appellavit, invitatur sponsa jam regis corpori juncta per baptismi sacramenta in vineam, id est credentium sibi plebem ex gentibus, et invenit eam producentem flores per bonum naturae, ad confidendum Deo coeli paratam. Sed calliditate insidiisque vulpium maturitatem fructus exspectantium ad demoliendam devorandamque parata, quae sunt perversarum animarum haereseos mordacissima dogmata ait Jesus suis doctoribus: « Capite nobis vulpes parvulas quae demo liuntur vineas, » id est convincite haereticos et schismaticos pravos fide, et dolosos verbo, qui dente pravae doctrinae rudes fidelium mentes lacerare solent. Nam vinea nostra floruit, id est sanctae electorum animae 600Dgerminant fructum fidei et bonorum operum late longeque. Verum duplici ratione intelligitur quod ait nostra, id est sive suam dixerit et patris, sive Ecclesiam sibi conjunctam participem dicat. Quam cum flores tam Ecclesiam ex Judaeis, quam maxima ex gentibus vidisset proferre, ante praeparat venatores vel custodes qui vulpes capiant quam flores maturos fructus pariant. De quorum numero est unus qui dicebat: Corripientes omnem hominem in captivitatem redigimus omnem scientiam extollentem se adversus scientiam Dei. Nam sive daemones vulpes intelligantur, sive haeretici et perversi doctores, per quos daemones loquuntur blasphemias, utrumque convenire manifestum est, per quos mentes 601Ahominum in quibus habitat recta fides Ecclesiae disterminantur. Quorum exterminium vel occultas insidias cavere sollicite debemus, et Deum ex toto corde diligere ut diligi ab eo mereamur: ideoque Ecclesia rectissime subjungit:

« Dilectus meus mihi, et ego illi. » Conjuncta itaque Ecclesia Christo vel anima verbo Dei ut eam abundantiore gaudio cumulet per omnia, et in quibus ipse per laetitiam pascitur, et quid ille delectetur in ea ostendit, et quid illa respondere debeat pro tantis beneficiis docetur. Sponsus videlicet decoram faciem in ea desiderat, sicut superius innuit, quae nulla peccati nigredine sit maculata, non turpium verborum mendaciique absconsa, vel blasphemiarum raucedine voce horrida, sed dulcedinem suis laudibus 601Bresonantem reddit vicem, eadem Ecclesia ait: « Dilectus meus mihi, et ego illi. » Christus et ipsa, caput et corpus velut unus homo, et tanquam membra caput, ita diligendo sequitur ipsum, propterea taliter loquitur. Nullus enim dilectus et adjutor Ecclesiae, nisi Christus, et nullus Christi dilectus, nisi Ecclesia: quia nullus alius recte diligit Christum, nisi unica Ecclesia, et nulla alia ab eo diligitur, nisi ipsa. « Qui pascitur inter lilia, donec aspiret dies, et inclinentur umbrae. » Id est, qui pascitur munditia fidei et candore virtutum, donec venturi saeculi dies illius judicii verus adveniat, et oriatur lux et umbrae, id est errores praesentis saeculi, qui nos impediunt, justitiae sole transeant et inclinentur cum suis amatoribus in chaos inferni.

601C« Revertere: Similis esto, dilecte mi, capreae hinnuloque cervorum super montes Bethel. » Vox est ista supradictae Ecclesiae ad Christum: Obsecro, mi dilecte, ut saepius tuae visitationis adventu revertaris ad me, quia carnem quam de patriarcharum origine sumpsisti, resurgens a mortuis, super omnem coelestium montium ascendisti, ut laborem peregrinationes meae speculatione aeterna releves. Capream hoc in loco sicut superius praelibavimus carnem dicunt Christi, propter similitudinem carnis peccati. Hinnulus cervorum dicitur ipse, quia de stirpe patriarcharum genitus, qui post resurrectionem suam ad coelos remeans, superexaltatus est super montes Bethel, quae domus Dei dicitur, id est supernam patriam angelorum. Unde Psalmista ad eum loquens 601Dait: « Domine, Dominus noster, quam admirabile est nomen tuum in universa terra! Quam elevata est magnificentia tua super coelos! » (Psal. VIII.) Nec moralis siquidem admittendus est sensus. Unusquisque enim credentium Deo, ipse se fecit montem Dei per sanctam conversationem, aut montem diaboli perverse vivendo. Bethel enim domus Dei interpretatur, ut praefati sumus. Quicunque ergo ita exhibuerit vitam suam, ut in eo delectetur Spiritus sanctus inhabitare, mons efficitur Dei qui est Ecclesia Dei. Super haec ergo, si quando offensus peccatis populi prolongaverit, revertitur, similis efficitur cursu vel consuetudine capreae hinnuloque cervorum, quarum natura est, voce ante se in montibus resonante post tergum 602Aemissa dum obviam sibi opinantur occurrere, ad locum unde abscesserant, velociori cursu reverti. Et haec causa ut exasperata ab hostibus consuetam nolit derelinquere sedem. Sic et Christus Dominus noster, quamvis provocatus, quamvis exasperatus peccatis hominum, jejuniis et precibus revocatus, ad pietatis consuetam revertitur sedem. Et si aliquando provocari videtur, ut studia inquirendo eum merito augeantur, non debet quis diffidere nec ab inquirendo cessare. Unde sequitur:

CHAPTER II.

« I am the flower of the field, and the lily of the valleys. » As if it were said openly: I am the glory of the world and the glory of the humble. Although hell too may be understood, by way of surplus, and the place where Christ deigned to descend to rescue the patriarchs, as certain men understand it. But let us, these things having been tasted beforehand, lest we be charged with having been ignorant of the meaning of certain men, speak more richly, setting down the words of blessed Ambrose. Before his incarnation, he says, the Savior, speaking in the person of an angel to the partriarchis and to the prophets, alone, to whom none is like among the angels, the thrones and the dominations, to be marveled at by all, is proved to be the singular flower glittering in the whole field of the heavens. 587AWhich portion of the fragrance of his knowledge the patriarchs or the prophets bore as they were able, and the world itself always admonished mortals by teaching. All knowledge, through the prophets, through the powers of signs — by slaying the impious through the waters of the deluge, or the Sodomites through fire — whereby the unwise might understand wisdom, and the foolish proud might at some time have sense that a loftier judge abides above them, paying out just vengeance. But when through the sacrament of the incarnation he came down hither into the valley of tears, among the thorns and the fellowships of sinners, he testifies that he was made a lily. In which valley what else was rioting but the forests of thorns — the foulest religion of idolatry, of envy, of thefts and murders, 587Bof soothsaying and auguries, or of fornications, or of magic arts? In the midst of which Christ, shown forth in the fragrance of his teaching and in the whiteness of his example, daily sets free the throng of believers. Three things therefore delightful in themselves and exceedingly pleasing to the eyes the nature of the lily is proved to contain, that is, whiteness, fragrance, and a medicine when burnt with fire. Which three wonderful things the Lord is known to have brought by like reason into this valley of the world, that is, the abolishing of sin, the wiping away of the lie, the tempering refreshment of the generative fires, from which is born the love of preserving virginity. And whosoever in either sex restrain themselves from such things are kindled by the examples of the glorious assumed manhood and of the blessed Virgin Mary. And thus he is called the flower of the field and the lily of the valleys 587Cthrough the assumption of an immaculate body. Which sacrament was for the freeing of his people, whereby he might heal us in his diverse members from our diverse languors; and the wounds which had been inflicted by the demons with the fiery darts of fornications might be healed by his teaching; and those which with the keenness of wickedness were wounding his friend in the prophets and the doctors might be turned into the pleasantness of lilies, from which the Church, born again, shines out — and she is pricked with the most bitter goads, as he himself says in what follows:

« As the lily among thorns, so is my friend among the daughters. » These friends, therefore, among whom the Church stands as a lily among thorns, although it is not said with distinctness whose daughters they are, yet from the fact that they are compared to thorns we can perceive that they are called the offspring, not of God, but 587Dof the devil, through most wicked teaching. For among so many venomous thorns the Church dwells — the world, namely, of the Gentiles or of the heretics, who assail her; and lest she grow numb with idleness, she is permitted to be armed against enemies and to be assailed, so that after the fight her strength may appear. Of such the Lord says: « Beware of the leaven of the Pharisees (Matth. XVI), » which is the teaching of the heretics. [Vulg. a fermento Pharisaeorum et Sadducaeorum, "of the Pharisees and Sadducees"] And the Apostle (I Cor. VI): « What, he says, fellowship hath light with darkness? » Or what meaning hath the temple of God with idols? Or what communication hath Christ with Belial? [Vulg. II Cor. VI, 16 qui consensus templo Dei cum idolis, "what agreement hath the temple of God with idols"] And nearly in like manner Isaias says: « Go out, he says, from the midst of them, and be ye clean, you that carry the vessels of the Lord (Isa. LII). » [Vulg. Isa. LII, 11 exite de medio eius … mundamini qui fertis vasa Domini, "go out from the midst of her"; de medio eorum, "from the midst of them", is the reading of II Cor. VI, 17] But in that he says: « As the lily among thorns, so is my friend 588Aamong the daughters, » he shows not the reproach of the just, who are the members of the Church, but their praise, that he might teach us that it is exceedingly praiseworthy to live piously among the impious, and to be in no way drawn along by the thorny converse of the perverse, among whom the just man is proved to shine like a light in the darkness. As if it were said to the Church: Thou seekest rest, but remember the praise of the little bed, that thou art made whiter by the goads of tribulations, and shalt acquire greater fruits from preaching than from quiet. And well does he say: « As the lily among thorns, so is my friend among the daughters, » that is, as the lily among thorns keeps its own fragrance and whiteness, so the Church of the elect among depraved Christians and heretics, if she yield not to them, is fragrant with virtues. There follows:

588B« As the apple tree among the trees of the woods, so is my beloved among the sons. I sat down under his shadow, whom I desired, and his fruit was sweet to my throat. » As therefore before the mystery of the incarnation, « in many parts and in many ways, » according to the Apostle, « the Son of God spoke to our fathers (Hebr. I), » or was shown forth in the prophets, so after the aforesaid mystery he kept festival, bestowing many good things in diverse ways upon human nature; and he is compared to diverse things and to diverse persons, according to time and place, or occasion. [Vulg. Hebr. I, 1 multifariam et multis modis olim Deus loquens patribus in prophetis, "God, who at sundry times and in divers manners spoke in times past to the fathers by the prophets"] For according to David the prophet (Psal. LXVII) he is the mountain of God, for that the fullness of the divinity was to dwell in him. He is also the young calf putting forth horns, through the two Testaments. And hoofs, through the eight beatitudes, put forth from the feet of the four evangelists. 588CAnd the lamb taking away the sin of the world, through the imparting of the gentleness of the Holy Spirit, so that, while clothing others, he himself always abounds. He is also the beloved bundle of myrrh, and the cluster of cypress in the vineyards of Engaddi; and so, according to the place, the Church calls him the apple tree or the pomegranate, for that he is the tree of life, offering himself with a different savor to different persons. He is without doubt the pomegranate tree, which shelters and defends under the shadow of his protection the souls afflicted with the emptiness of hunger, wearied under the burning sun of wickedness, and with the fruit of the lips of his teaching refreshes by his sweetness those who hunger and thirst. From the expulsion from paradise up to the childbearing of the Virgin, the human offspring was afflicted with many labors and with intolerable assaults of the demons — which offspring is here, under the person of the Church, 588Dcalled the friend; and at the coming of him who said: « Come to me, all you that labor and are burdened, and I will make you rest (Matth. XI), » [Vulg. et ego reficiam vos, "and I will refresh you"] resting under the covering of his protection, believing in one God, she says with gladness: « I sat down under his shadow, whom I desired. » Which sweetness of the kingdom of the heavens — although present protection may be understood, and she names the rest of the blessed — or else, tasting his body and his blood, which confer eternal life, she says with exultation: « And his fruit was sweet to my throat. » It may be that it was uttered so that the sweetness of the divine Scriptures should be understood in the holy preachers, who are called the throats of the Lord. Of which sweetness the Prophet says: « Taste, he says, and see that the Lord is sweet. » 589A« Blessed is the man, etc. (Psal. XXXIII). » And that: « How sweet are thy words to my palate! O Lord, more than honey to my mouth (Psal. CXVIII). » From the fruit of this tree, as one languishing in the last infirmity of despair, the throng of believers was restored at his coming by the juice of the pomegranate; which fruit is shown to be poured daily into the throat of the Church through those who act in Christ's stead; and thus through the several degrees of advancement she is led in to the hidden understanding of the divine law, that she may be able to recognize all those things which were done in the Old Testament. For she says: « As the apple among the trees of the woods, so is my beloved among the sons. » As if it were said: As the apple goes before all the trees of the wood in sight, in fragrance and in taste, so Christ goes before all the saints who are called the sons of God. Of whom it is said: 589B« Then shall all the trees of the woods rejoice before the face of the Lord, because he cometh (Psal. XCV). » Whence they are called sons, not by malice, as the daughters are, who are compared to thorns. There is indeed great exultation to the trees of the woods, while they behold that such a tree has risen up in their midst, whose fruit has adorned the whole forest, has taken away all the languors of souls, and has sweetened all the fruits of bitterness. Under whose shadow, all the heat of sadness and all the weight of sin being cast off, the Church tells of the rest long desired and now found, and, fattened with the sweetest fruit, of the joys obtained for growing souls. « Under the shadow which I desired, she says, I sat down, and his fruit was sweet to my throat. » As if she said: With him protecting me, whom I always sought to have present, I remain quiet and secure, 589Cand by his grace he has made me a partaker of heavenly sweetness: and thus she testifies that she is led in, in due order, to the understanding of higher mysteries, saying:

« The king brought me into the wine cellar, he set in order charity in me. » [Vulg. introduxit me in cellam vinariam, without rex, "the king"] The wine cellar ought to be understood as the Church, as not a few suppose, in whose unity alone the Holy Spirit is wont to be given and received. Whose grace is designated in this place by the name of wine. Wine — since, when the apostles had received the Holy Spirit, the Jews said: « These men are full of new wine (Act. II). » In which cellar charity has been set in order, that each may love God with his whole heart more than himself, and his neighbor as himself. Although the wine cellar may, by way of surplus, be understood as the mystery of Christ's passion, into 589Dwhich the Church has been led, when through faith she deserved to share in his sacraments. But because she testifies that she has been led in, in due order, to the mysteries to be understood by growing souls, of necessity she wishes the wine cellar to be understood as the knowledge and the sweetness of the divine law. To which sweetness she invites the young maidens, and by praising it urges them to hasten — the sweetness which Christ taught by giving it to drink, when he said: « Labor for the food which perisheth not, but which endureth unto eternity (Joan VI). » [Vulg. operamini non cibum qui perit sed qui permanet in vitam aeternam, "labor not for the meat which perisheth, but for that which endureth unto life everlasting" — the received text sets the negation on cibum] Those set in this work the Prophet calls blessed, saying: « Blessed are they that search his testimonies, that seek him with their whole heart (Psal. CXVIII). » For there are testimonies of the law in the Old Testament which prove that Christ, the Redeemer of the human race, was to come, has come, 590Aand will come again in glory. Let us therefore set down the law as a most spacious palace, in which Christ the king dwells, the Word of the almighty Father, where the souls of believers are led in to the eternal warfare; where she says that she has been led in — into that same understanding of the law, namely — to consider the marvels which God wrought through the several patriarchs or prophets up to the childbearing of the Virgin, which she has now figuratively named the wine cellar; where, being led in, she received into herself the order of charity. In which order of charity, what else is to be believed that she is first imbued with, save that she should believe and acknowledge that first the bread ought to be named, in whom the Son always is, that he may invoke him as the Word; second the Son, in whom the Father always is; third the Holy Spitum, who by true reason is proved to proceed, as from voice and word, from 590Bthe Father and the Son? Who through all is the one God to be loved, according to that saying at the beginning of the Decalogue: « Thou shalt love the Lord thy God with thy whole heart. » The second order is, « with thy whole soul. » The third order is, « with thy whole strength (Exod. XX). » Then already in the several precepts of the law charity sets in order how, or with what affection, the neighbor is to be loved, or the several persons in the Church. For by one order is reciprocal charity to be weighed out to God, who according to the Apostle (I Joan. IV) first loved us, who is proved to have conferred upon us the whole sum of charity through Christ. By another order to a faithful friend, by another order to a dearest son, by another order it is to be paid to parents, through whom we are who were not; by another order to a full brother, by another order to a spouse; by another order the servant 590Cto his master, by another order the master to his servant, by another to a stranger, by another to an acquaintance, by another to a priest, by another to kinsmen and neighbors. For to bestow necessary things upon a needy sick man, or upon a stranger, not for the display of human praise but for the sake of eternal recompense, is the token of ordered charity. To console the needy and those oppressed by unjust judgment, to visit the sick or those consigned to prison, is shown to be ordered charity. In no business to stand in awe of a man's person against the truth, mercifully to admonish the unwise, most truthfully to rebuke the proud, is the manifest order of charity. But whoever loves father, or mother, wife, sons, or brothers, or the riches of the present time, more than God, there is indeed charity in him, but not ordered, since above all things we ought to love him 590Dand our neighbor as ourselves. This is the order of charity. The Son of God therefore has set charity in order in his Church, that every son of the Church may know in what order of charity he is to render his duty to each, saying in the Gospel: « Render the things that are Caesar's to Caesar, and the things that are God's to God (Matth. XXII). » And through the Apostle he says: « Render to each one his dues: to whom honor, honor; to whom fear, fear; to whom tribute, tribute; to whom custom, custom (Rom. XII). » [Vulg. Rom. XIII, 7 reddite omnibus debita, "render to all their dues", and the received order runs tributum … vectigal … timorem … honorem] Not all who are called by the Christian name are led in through this order of charity into the aforesaid understanding which she named the wine cellar, but he who shall have imitated, however little, Paul, who said (II Cor. XI): I have labored more than 591Aall, in watchings, in fastings, and the rest: « and I have counted all things, he says, as dung, that I may gain Christ (Phil. III). » At the head of this canticle the Church, led into the bedchamber or the storeroom of the king, gives praise, where, having entered, she says, « We will be glad and rejoice in thee, » having recognized, namely, his bodily presence, who is the wisdom of the Father, who lay hidden as in an inner chamber amid the heralds of the prophets, in whom are all exultations and gladnesses and treasures. But here she says that she is led in to the understanding of the divine law and to the learning of the order of charity, and that she does not rejoice and exult, but languishes, for that, according to Solomon (Eccle. I): « He that addeth knowledge, addeth sorrow. » [Vulg. Eccle. I, 18 qui addit scientiam addit et laborem, "he that addeth knowledge, addeth also labour"] And whoever by pursuing has begun to be made nearer to wisdom, by so much does he scarcely behold her, drawn far off. 591BFrom which sorrow is born the blessed love of Christ, and from love a more glorious soundness, the languor of the soul, which withdraws from her all her powers for sinning. And though she has attained the hidden mysteries of the manifold wisdom of God, though she holds the citadel of perfection, yet she does not believe that the sum of the aforesaid sentence or understanding alone suffices for her, unless she be compassed with apples and stayed up with diverse flowers. Whence she straightway subjoined:

« Stay me up with flowers, compass me about with apples, because I languish with love. » She seeks the bodily presence of the wisdom of God the Father, that is, Christ, in the cellar of the knowledge of the law; but because she is not yet permitted to see him whom she desires as he is, she demands the examples of others for her consolation, whereby she may be able meanwhile to temper her languor. For she says: 591C« Stay me up with flowers, compass me about with apples, » that is, console me with the examples either of those beginning a good work, or of those bringing it to perfection: because as yet, in the weariness of my pilgrimage, I languish with the love of the vision above. Whence not undeservedly by the flowers are understood the souls already beginning a good work and giving out the fragrance of heavenly desire; by the apples from the flowers, the minds already perfect in good things, which come to the fruit of a good work from the beginning of a holy purpose. She therefore who languishes with love seeks to be stayed up with flowers, to be compassed about with apples, because she is still not permitted to see none whom she desires; it is a great consolation if she rejoice at the advances of others, so that the soul which is not yet able to contemplate the face of God may rest in the good work of her neighbor. Let us see what the mind of Paul was, who said: « To me to live 591Dis Christ, and to die is gain (Phil. I). » Hence it is that again he says: « I have a desire to be dissolved and to be with Christ, a thing by much the better (Ibid.). » But behold, thou who desirest to be dissolved, let us see with what love thou languishest. Because meanwhile thou seest thyself put off, dost thou not seek, I pray, to be stayed up with flowers? Plainly thou dost seek it: for there follows: « But to abide in the flesh, I judge necessary for you (Ibid.). » [Vulg. Phil. I, 24 permanere autem in carne magis necessarium est propter vos, "but to abide in the flesh is more needful for you"] And to his advancing disciples he says: « For what is our hope or joy, or crown of glory? Are not you, before our Lord Jesus Christ? » (I Thes. II.) The soul therefore, joined to the Word of God, led into the understanding of the law, running through the several points of Scripture, as has often been said, and tasting the savors of the several vessels of the wine cellar 592Aof the divine books, as though inebriated with the wine of the gladness of the beatitude always to come, although she does not discern the bridegroom in the present, says that she languishes with love, because she comes to him but slowly. With that love, without doubt, of which the Prophet says: « My soul hath thirsted after God, the living fount; when shall I come and appear before the face of the Lord? » (Psal. XLI.) [Vulg. ad Deum fortem vivum, "after the strong living God", and ante faciem Dei, "before the face of God"] Through which love of knowledge is germinated the love of eternal life, and from the love of eternal life the endurance of persecution. Savoring which love, as though placed upon a little bed, she besought that she might be embraced by the left hand of promise and the right hand of Christ's help, saying:

« His left hand is under my head, and his right hand shall embrace me. » For what is designated by the left hand but the present life, or the temporal gifts 592Bof God almighty? And what by the right hand, but everlasting life is figured? For the left hand of God — the Church, namely — has placed the prosperity of the present life and its temporal gifts as it were under her head, which she presses down with the intention of the highest love. But the right hand of God embraces her, because she is held with whole devotion beneath his eternal blessedness. Since here the bridegroom strengthens the mind through hope, and there glorifies it through recompense. Or by the left hand the Old Testament, and by the right hand the New Testament, is intimated. For it can be so understood, that the left hand of Christ sustains the soul on the left, lest by the hooks of the incentives of the vices she be led astray on the left, but that, being always displayed with hidden compunctions, she be lifted up to the kingdom. And lest, uplifted into pride, she make a fall on the right, in which the devil fell, he embraces her with his right hand by permitting tribulations 592Cand manifold restraints, and by helping her when she is wearied. It is also of force, by way of surplus, to understand it thus: that the left hand under the head is the shield of faith, which the fighter holds in his left hand, and that the embrace of the right hand is understood as the sword of prayer, with which he is always armed on the right, so that by the one kind of arms the adversary may be repelled, and by the other laid low. There follows:

« I adjure you, O daughters of Jerusalem, by the roes and the harts of the fields, that you stir not up, nor make the beloved to awake, till she please. » After many labors, namely of the Church, and many runnings to and fro, through which she is rendered anxious and, wearied within the holy wine cellar, is given over to slumber, the daughters 592Dof Jerusalem are adjured not to stir her up, lest, awakened from sleep, she be too quickly given back to her former labors; and by this they are adjured, that she may be loved with firm charity. By the roes, namely, and the harts of the fields, which are given to be understood as souls of no small merit or labor, and by which the daughters of Jerusalem are adjured. For among the Christian people souls are compared to diverse animals according to the quality of their merits. Our Lord Jesus Christ therefore has a palace, which is understood to contain within itself many storerooms, and a wine cellar, and the spaces of the fields. Whose flowers blessed Paul, having entered, praises in wonder: « O the depth, he says, of the riches of the wisdom and of the knowledge of God! (Rom. XI.) » In which palace, among many riches, that is, the multitude of the saints, he is understood to have also diverse souls, 593Ajust as in a great house, according to the Apostle (II Thes. II), there are not only vessels of gold and of silver, but also of earth and of wood. Whence not undeservedly the daughters of Jerusalem are the souls of the elect, contemplating the vision of peace of the country above. But the spiritual roes or harts he calls the loaves of the Old and the New Testament, for which the multitude of the sacred Scriptures is the pasture. Or else certain men will have those souls understood as roes which, by the keenness of dialectic, by the sense of understanding, have been accustomed to range about through philosophy and with the mind — which souls, now enclosed within the aforesaid palace by the net of the hunters' art, that is, of the apostles and of the other doctors, taken by their hand, their religion being changed for the better, range over the fields of the divine Scriptures, or, interpreting whatever is steep and obscure in wisdom, range over the 593Bmountains; and the worship of the gods is daily turned into the praise of the Creator. And they show the swiftness of their course in faith, and with keenness of sense, after the manner of roes, they look out from afar upon the ambushes of their adversaries. It is the nature of roes to have the front part of the body proved higher, the hinder part lower. And for this cause, always ascending upward, it is found swifter in its course upon the mountain, by which route the adversary is the lesser in pursuing. But harts appear to be understood as those who, in the very swiftness of their philosophy of teaching, confessed not many gods, but one God invisible, immense, inestimable, the Creator of all things, filling the whole world everywhere — who themselves also were taken by the holy doctors with the net of preaching into the bosom of the Church. Who, dwelling in the steep places of life, 593Cafter the manner of harts seek the fount of waters, destroy serpents, and, like clean animals, chew the cud. These therefore are without doubt the harts of the fields and the roes, that is, the souls of faithful philosophers, converted from the unbelief of superstitions, by whom the bridegroom conjures the daughters of Jerusalem to suffer the bride to rest a little in the sleep of rest — souls which delight to run together with the Church in those works in which they themselves were exalted to heavenly glory, that through them, the bodily burden being laid aside, they may feast with those souls in the heavens. Whom the Lord Christ bids to rest in the body, for the gain or the consolation of other souls, till she herself please, until the bridegroom himself, looking to her advantage, and she herself, may know her own strength: because without him she can do nothing. 593DBut it can be understood, according to the former understanding, where we said that the roes and harts signify the Fathers of the Old and the New Testament, in this way: I call to witness the peaceable — as if it were said, the souls of the faithful — by their several virtues, which were signified by the clean and cud-chewing animals, that they hinder not by any importunity the pious pursuits of the brethren, but that each so rejoice at the advancement of his neighbors as at his own. Otherwise Christ admonishes that no one of the doctors frighten off, by gifts, namely, or by threats, those who are about to believe, but that they preach only the things that are holy, and lead those about to believe, or those converted, willingly to the faith or to religion, and disturb them not in their own tranquillity, until, driven by divine inspiration, they gird themselves to the pursuits of a loftier life. But the perfect 594Asoul of the elect, and, so to say, holy Church, in the very slumber of rest always hears him by fearing him, always sees him by loving him. Whence now she says — it is to be supplied — this is he whom I heard adjuring the daughters of Jerusalem, that they should not stir me up as I rested in his embrace. Or else his voice is the evangelical preaching. And what this voice of the beloved of the Church may say, he declares in the Gospel itself with his own mouth. « See, he says, that you be not seduced by false prophets. For many shall come in my name, saying that I am Christ, and shall seduce many, and shall give signs, so that even, if it be possible, the elect be led into error (Matth. XXIV). » [Vulg. Matt. XXIV, 4 videte ne quis vos seducat, "take heed that no man seduce you"; a pseudoprophetis, "by false prophets", is not in the received text] And: « See that your hearts be not made heavy with surfeiting or drunkenness (Luc. XXI). » And: « Be like to servants who wait for their lord, when 594Bhe shall return from the wedding (Luc. XII). » [Vulg. Luc. XII, 36 similes hominibus expectantibus dominum suum, "like to men who wait for their lord"] Lest the Church, resting in the embraces of the bridegroom, be deceived under the appearance of doctors. And lest she grow numb with idleness after her labors, or be unstrung by delights, she is thus admonished in the Gospel. But in the very rest of her security let her be solicitous always to lend the ear of her heart to the voice of Christ — that is, in the divine Scriptures — and let her study to give her labor to assiduous prayer. Those therefore who are set over the Christian people are admonished by these things to announce to the people without intermission what this voice of the beloved of the Church enjoins: how, namely, he came from the bosom of the Father into human fellowship, that the Word might be made flesh; how, according to Habacuc the prophet (Cap. III), humbling the pride of the demons, he broke the mountains of violence, and melted the everlasting hills, treading down under the feet of believers 594CSatan, leaping across the aforesaid mountains. And weighing this, the Church says:

« Behold he cometh leaping upon the mountains, skipping over the hills. » Wilt thou hear what leaps he made? Such leaps, I say, did my beloved make. From heaven he came into the womb, from the womb into the manger, from the manger he came to the cross, from the cross he came into the sepulchre, from the sepulchre he returned into heaven: he it is who was raised up also above all mountains and hills, that is, the Fathers of the New and of the Old Testament, who by the merit of their virtues and by the summit of their faith are more eminent than the rest. In these the Lord is said not only to dwell, but to leap across, because he appeared incomparable not only in divinity, but in humanity. Or else through the apostles, as we began to say above, he melted all the pride of the wisdom of the world lifting itself up 594Dagainst the Creator, and, brought to nothing, it is humbled. In whom shone not rhetorical arguments, but the power of the Holy Spirit; and, the throng of demons laid low under the feet of believers, he was shown to the Church to have come into the world leaping upon the mountains and skipping over the hills, and he conversed among men. Whence also there is presently subjoined:

« My beloved is like a roe and a young hart of the harts. » We understand Christ compared to a roe not by any diminishing of the divinity, who is always the same, but through the mystery of the incarnation, namely because he appeared in the likeness of sinful flesh, in which the nature of the flesh, received into heaven, obtained increase. For some think he was likened to a roe for this reason also, 595Athat the illumination of our salvation, taking away the blindness of the heart, showed itself by coming in the flesh in the latter part of the age — which illumination he had shown to Moses when he asked for it on Mount Sinai. Of which mystery David says: « The wings of the dove are silvered over, unto the hinder parts of his back (Psal. LXVII). » Just as the hinder part of the body of roes shows whiteness; and because many, not only of the Jews but also of the philosophers of the Gentiles, believed through the humility of his incarnation — by whom are understood those wild souls, once cleaner than the rest of those mentioned above, which through his teaching were gathered into the fellowship of the one true Israel. To such, according to the opinion of some, he is proclaimed like, in the voice of the Church, who for the innocent and for the multitude of believers is compared to a young hart of the harts. 595BHe is called a young hart of the harts for this reason, that he was begotten of the stock of the patriarchs, who are designated by the name of harts; or else a young hart of the harts on account of the form of his humility, the horns of his power not yet being lifted up, with which at the second coming he will plead against the world, of which it was said in the psalm: Then « the horns of the just shall be exulted (Psal. LXXIV). » [Vulg. cornua iusti exaltabuntur, "the horns of the just shall be exalted"] For the young hart of the harts is without horns, and shows in itself the love of its own aspect rather than terribleness, as our Lord Christ is known to have done at his first coming. Of whose first coming there is presently subjoined: « Behold he standeth behind our wall, looking back through the windows, looking forth through the lattices. » [Vulg. despiciens per fenestras, "looking down through the windows"] Clothed with the wall of our mortality, he lay hidden as to the substance of his divinity. But looking forth toward us 595Cthrough the lattices and the windows while he wrought miracles, so that he might appear from his miracles who lay hidden from his sufferings. For he who showed to human eyes that which he assumed of mortal nature, and in his own self, who remained invisible, stood as it were behind a wall for those seeking to see him in the open, because he did not offer himself to be seen with majesty made manifest. For he stood as it were behind a wall, who showed the nature of the humanity which he assumed, and hid the nature of the divinity from human eyes. For whoever looks back through windows or lattices is neither wholly seen nor wholly not seen. For our Redeemer, because he both did divine things and endured human things, came to men as it were through lattices and windows: that as God he might appear from his miracles, as has been said, and lie hidden from his sufferings, and be discerned as man from his sufferings. 595DRightly is it said: « Behold he standeth behind our wall, » and the rest. It can, if it please anyone, be understood thus: Behold he himself who was crucified, who with the right hand of his divinity held the Church languishing, that is, mourning in the apostles, in the embrace of consolation, with whom he was buried in the flesh — with her indeed he stands, rising again, behind the wall of our unbelief, which we had built with muddy works by sinning. Of which he says through Isaias the prophet: « Is not my hand strong to deliver? But your sins have made a wall between you and God (Isa. L). » [Vulg. Isa. L, 2 numquid adbreviata et parvula facta est manus mea ut non possim redimere, "is my hand shortened and become little, that I cannot redeem?"; the second clause conflates Isa. LIX, 2 iniquitates vestrae diviserunt inter vos et Deum vestrum, "your iniquities have divided between you and your God"] Standing behind which, the Lord Christ waits to be called upon by the impious, and calls the sinful soul to penance, waits to be believed by the unbelieving. Behind which wall he 596Astands, until the time of baptism or of penance. And though we do not deserve to look upon him, yet by this, that we renounce idolatry, we make windows in the aforesaid wall, through which Christ looks back upon us, defending us from unclean spirits. And by bending the knees of the heart to him alone we make lattices, through which he may look forth upon our compunction as we turn to him. But when we have come to the true conversion of the aforesaid baptism, or of penance, he takes it away out of the midst, pardon of sins being granted, and speaks to us, saying: « Come to me, all you that labor and are burdened, and I will refresh you (Matth. XI). » And: « Come, ye blessed of my Father, possess the kingdom prepared for you from the foundation of the world (Matth. XXV). » Even as the master of the Gentiles, Paul, taught that it was done at his coming. 596B« Who took away, he says, the wall of the fence from the midst, and reconciled us to God the Father through his own blood (Ephes. II). » [Vulg. Ephes. II, 14-16 medium parietem maceriae solvens … ut reconciliet ambos in uno corpore Deo per crucem, "breaking down the middle wall of partition … that he might reconcile both to God in one body by the cross"] Whence, when the Church was in a manner shut in within the enclosures of walls for fear of persecution, after his resurrection, while the Apostles were afraid and cast down with exceeding terror, he entered, the doors being shut, and brought great confidence and great joy, saying: « Peace be to you. It is I, fear not (Luc. XXIV). » Then was fulfilled that which follows:

« My beloved speaks to me: Arise, make haste, my love, my dove, my beautiful one, and come. » He speaks to her, that is, by calling her to the perfection of life, by telling her no longer to fear the author of death, the devil, nor to remain any longer within the 596Cbarriers of unbelief and of torpor. « Make haste, » he says, « and come, » because I by dying have given back the life that was lost. Now be no longer terrified by death, whose empire I have destroyed. For he was standing behind our wall, because the sun of justice stood in our way with his own shadow, of whom the prophet had promised, saying: « The sun of justice shall rise for you that fear God, and health in his wings (Mal. IV). » [Vulg. timentibus nomen meum, "that fear my name"] He speaks to the Church in the apostles when, rising from the dead, he consoles them, saying: « Be of good cheer, for I have overcome the world (Joan. XVI). » And: « All power is given to me in heaven and in earth (Matth. XXVIII). » And: « Fear not them that kill the body, but cannot kill the soul (Matth. XI). » Whence by this saying she is summoned to the office of preaching, as she herself says: « my beloved speaks 596Dto me, » that is, he exhorts me to preach, saying: « Arise, make haste, my love, and come, » that is, arise from the couch of repose, on which you seek to take care of yourself alone; make haste, and come, to fulfil also the care of your neighbour's salvation; spend your zeal on the office of diligent preaching. For he also grants confidence by affable words when he adds:

« For winter is now past, the rain is gone and departed. The flowers have appeared in our land, the time of pruning is come. » He teaches her, that is, no longer to fear at all the savagery of wicked men, nor to dread the terrors of the demons, because he has both reconciled God the Father to the Church by the offering of the flesh he assumed, and has hamstrung the army of the demons by the cross, 597Aand after the wintry and icy harshness has shown the flowery time of his springtime coming, and — as to how great and of what sort would be the rewards of joys that he was to display to her by his presence — has told what was still to come as though it were already done. Whence it is also said to her: « Rise up, make haste, my love, my beautiful one, and come. » For she becomes his love by acknowledging God; beautiful, by keeping the humility of Christ. And she is made a dove by requiring nothing of earthly desires beyond the meanest food and the clothing of the body, but, always holding the simplicity of doves, she joins herself to the Holy Spirit. Or she is called love through charity; beautiful or fair through the comeliness of the virtues and the laver of regeneration; a dove through the pouring-in of the Holy Spirit. As therefore when spring comes on, the forerunner of summer, which puts winter to flight, 597Bwhen every creature rejoices restored to life, and all animals after their kind are embodied for offspring, and being made pregnant build their lairs, and the birds put together their nests, and call one another with their distinct voices and movements; when now the ground prepares banquets for creeping things, lest food be lacking to the feathered creatures; when the tuneful voice of the flying things resounds sweetly in melody and pipes, and the bee, armed with wings, goes forth to plunder the flowers: so our Lord Jesus Christ, after the rough winter of idolatry and philosophic teaching, adorned with the flower of springtime — which is our Pasch, the passing from death to life — through his passion the face of the world of martyrs, or of all holy works, and the beginnings of the faith of believers. At that time he calls the Church from the couch of repose to the office 597Cof preaching, nay rather from the valley of tears to the mountain of God's paradise, by the example of his own death. In short, when he had said, « Arise, make haste, my love, my beautiful one, and come, » he presently added, « for winter is now past, » that is, the power of darkness, which made the world sorrowful, passes from this world to hell, now that Christ the sun of justice is come.
