Metit myrrham cum aromatibus, quoniam martyres cum caeteris electis ad maturitatem 612Bpraemiorum perducit. « Comedi favum cum melle meo, et bibi vinum meum cum lacte meo. » In favo et vino praedicatores, in melle et lacte auditores intelliguntur, et utrisque internus judex est qui probat et remunerat. « Comedite, amici, et bibite, et inebriamini, charissimi. Ego dormio, et cor meum vigilat. » Vox est Christi ad Ecclesiam. Ac si aperte dicat: Amici faciendo quae praecipio. Charissimi, integra charitate amplectendo. Obsecro itaque factis sanctorum, quasi epulis praecipuis, praecordia vestra replete. Cui respondit Ecclesia: « Ego dormio et cor meum vigilat, » id est, donante gratia Dei, in pace praesentis vitae eum colo: et quo tranquillius ab incursibus internis vaco, eo altius video quam bonus est Deus. Moraliter vigilanti corde dormit 612Cquis, qui per hoc quod interius contemplando proficit, ab inquieto foris opere quiescit. Sed inter haec sciendum est, quia quandiu in hac mortali carne vivitur, nullus ita in contemplationis virtute proficit, ut in ipso jam incircumscripto luminis radio mentis oculos infigat. Neque enim omnipotens Deus jam in sua claritate conspicitur, sed quiddam sub illa speculatur anima, unde refota proficiat, et post ad visionis ejus gloriam pertingat. Potest et haec vox ad apostolos intelligi: « Comedite et bibite. » Ac si diceret: Sit nobis laetitia vel refectio de conversione populorum, in vestram transeuntium unitatem.

« Vox dilecti mei pulsantis: Aperi mihi, soror mea, amica mea, columba mea, immaculata mea. » Pulsat Dominus, quando quae bona sunt per 612Dsanctam docet doctrinam. Vel ostium cordis nostri pulsat, cum nos ad profectum virtutum excitat. Aperit enim Ecclesia, cum intima cordis sui illi patefacit. Soror vocatur, quia cohaeres per sanguinem Christi. Amica, quia est arcanis illius conscia sive per dilectionem, quia servat quae jubentur. Columba, quia Spiritus sancti dono illustrata. Immaculata, quia sola aspectu Dei digna. « Quia caput meum plenum est rore, et cincinni capitis mei guttis noctium. » Caput Christi, Deus. Cincinni sunt fidelium collectiones. In rore et guttis noctium, frigescens charitas in multis ostenditur, quam in Deum et proximos habere debuerunt. Ideo necessario excitat Dominus fideles quosque ad praedicationis 613Astudium. Cui provocanti ad laborem docendi respondit Ecclesia:

« Exspoliavi me tunica mea, quomodo induar illa? » Ac si aperte dicat: Deserui negotia saecularia tui causa, quomodo repetam illa? « Lavi pedes meos, quomodo inquinabo illos? » Id est jam secretae compunctionis fletibus ablui cogitationes terrenas, quomodo mundi sordibus iterum polluar? Quia praedicationis officium sine occupatione saeculari vix potest esse. Aliter: Sacerdotes in Ecclesia, qui in ipsa congregatione fidelium, sacramenta visibilia ministrant, velut quidam cincinni in ornamento describuntur. Sed quia plerumque mortalitatis hujus vicus, vel tenebrarum operibus obfuscantur, velut guttis noctium praegravati, Christo quasi adhaerere 613Bvidentur. Sed quia eumdem Christum non sincere annuntiant, propterea pro talibus ingemiscens subsequitur. « Spoliavi me tunica mea, quomodo induar illa? Lavi pedes meos, quomodo inquinabo illos? » hoc est, veniens ad fidem jam veterem hominem secundum pristinam conversationem deposui, et indui novum, et si quid contagii, dum in terrenis ambularem, pedibus meis accessit, Christo lavante, ablutum est, et nunc ministros sacramentorum per quos ordinari debeam, carnalibus infectos vitiis conspicio. Nunquid aequum est rursum per eorum exempla male vivendo inquinari a pristinis me patiar malis?

« Dilectus meus misit manum suam per foramen. » Manum quippe suam dilectus per foramen mittit, 613Ccum nos Dominus occulta invisibiliter compunctione ad opus virtutum accendit, atque per arctam et angustam viam ad regna coelorum transire docet, nobisque in memoriam revocat quomodo et quanta de sinu patris descendens pro nobis esset passus. « Et venter meus intremuit ad tactum ejus. » Haec recolens intima conscientia sponsa, tota expavescens, pigritiam quietis accusans ad laborem praedicationis festinat. Moraliter: Dilectus manum suam per foramen mittit, quando virtute sua Dominus nostrum animum per subtilem intellectum pulsat. Et venter in tactu illius contremiscit, quia infirmitas nostra per hoc quod coelestis gaudii intellectu tangitur, ipsa sua exsultatione turbatur, et fit in mente pavor in laetitia, quia jam sentit quod de coelesti 613Dgaudio diligat, et adhuc metuit ne non percipiat, quod vix tenuiter sentit. Quid igitur restat, nisi quod sequitur:

« Surrexi ut aperirem dilecto meo: Manus meae distillaverunt myrrham, et digiti mei pleni myrrha probatissima. » Vox est sponsae. Surgere est ad Christum corde evigilare. Aperire est verbum Dei praedicare, vel puram confusionem proferre. In manibus opera, in digitis discretio signatur, sive martyres. In myrrha mortificatio carnis, sive passiones. Quae tunc probatissima est cum solummodo pro charitate Dei et proximi, vel continentia fit, vel passiones fiunt

« Pessulum ostii mei aperui dilecto meo. » Ecclesia 614Apessulum sponso aperuit: quia templum sui pectoris divina visitatione et inhabitatione dignum facit. « At ille declinaverat atque transierat. » Quia nulli in hac vita plena visio Dei, sicut in futuro conceditur, ideo transire dicitur, quia in futuro se videndum et perfruendum plenius ostendit. « Anima mea liquefacta est, ut dilectus locutus est. » Perseverat Ecclesia in admiratione sui stuporis. Quanto suavius, inquit, vocem atque propinquitatem dilecti mei accepi, tanto sublimius quidquid in me erat frigidum, charitate incaluit, et quidquid rigidum, liquefuit. « Quaesivi illum et non inveni. » Quia donum compunctionis et dulcedinis intimae non arbitrio est volenti, sed in miseratione dantis. Ideo non semper habent illud aequaliter, quia non ita se offert Deus 614Bin exsilio laborantibus, quomodo in patria regnantibus.

« Invenerunt me custodes qui circumeunt civitatem, percusserunt me et vulneraverunt me, tulerunt pallium meum mihi custodes murorum. » Custodes civitatis, id est Ecclesiae sancti doctores sunt, qui sedula praedicatione circumeunt corda singulorum, et spiculis coelestis amoris vulnerant, et ut magis ardeant inflammescunt, et vetustae conversationis tegmen eis subtrahunt. Possunt et per custodes civitatis saeculi hujus intelligi potestates, qui rem publicam velut quamdam civitatem regere noscuntur. Hi enim electam Ecclesiam in primordiis Christo credentem comprehendentes, plagis et suppliciis illam devastaverunt, sacerdotesque et ministros 614Cillius interimentes, quasi quoddam pallium ornamenti sui tulerunt.

« Adjuro vos, filiae Hierusalem, si inveneritis dilectum meum, ut nuntietis ei quia amore langueo. » Quae sunt filiae Hierusalem, crebro dictum est: merito languet sponsa, cum gladio verbi Dei percussa, actionis terrenae exuit amictum. Et filias Hierusalem, id est Deo dignas animas adjurat, ut sui amoris magnitudinem ad Deum referant, et ad ejus videndam gloriam supernum sibi poscant auxilium.

« Qualis est dilectus tuus ex dilecto, o pulcherrima mulierum, qualis est dilectus tuus ex dilecto, quia sic adjurasti nos? » Vox sanctorum praedicatorum 614Dinterrogantium et fidem Ecclesiae ex ejus responsione inquirentium, id est obsecro te, quia sic adjurasti me, ut amorem quo te languescere dicis mihi ostendas, et qualis sit dilectus tuus ex dilecto, id est Filius ex Patre, Deus ex Deo, et ex ea qua possit parte diligi et non timeri, manifestes. quoniam « perfecta charitas foras mittit timorem. »

« Dilectus meus candidus, et rubicundus, electus ex millibus. » Dilectus Ecclesiae, Christus candidus est, quia sine peccato. Aliter: Candidus dicitur, secundum id quod « in principio erat Verbum, et Verbum erat apud Deum, et Deus erat Verbum. » Rubicundus, quia « Verbum caro factum est et habitavit in nobis. » Vel candidus in virginitate, rubicundus 615Ain sanguine passionis. Electus ex millibus, quia solus mediator Dei et hominum.

« Caput ejus aurum optimum. » Caput Christi Deus. Sicut inter metalla nihil melius auro, ita nec in creaturis quidquam comparabitur Creatori, quia solus bonus est et optimus. « Comae ejus sicut elatae palmarum, nigrae quasi corvus. » Comae Christi catervae sunt sanctorum, quae Deo fideli famulatu adhaerent. Elatae palmae, juxta illud Apostoli: « Conversatio nostra in coelis est (Phil. III): » Nigrae propter despectionem apud homines, vel quia peccatores se esse testantur. Possunt et elatae ob victoriam: nigrae ob pressuras dici: Laudavit sponsus pulchritudinem sponsae, idcirco reddit sponsa vices et laudat eum.

615B« Oculi ejus sicut columbae super rivulos aquarum. » Id est sancti doctores per quos Ecclesia videt quae recta sunt: Qui bene columbae propter simplicitatem et innocentiam comparantur, et rivulis aquarum propter charismata divina. « Quae lacte sunt lotae, et resident juxta fluenta plenissima. » Lacte lotas dicit, id est gratia dulcissima mundatas, sive per baptismum purificatas. Fluenta plenissima omnium donorum spiritualium est abundantia. Potest in rivulis veteris legis eruditio, et in fluentis plenissima perfectio evangelicae doctrinae designari, quia sancti praedicatores de thesauris suis proferunt nova et vetera (Matth. XIII).

« Genae illius sicut areolae aromatum, consitae a pigmentariis. » In genis Salvatoris nostri ejus modesta 615Cpietas, simul et severitas exprimitur. Areolae aromatum virtutes et dulcedo et fama gloriae ejus designatur. Consitae a pigmentariis, id est prophetis, apostolis, futura incarnationis ejus arcana, et illius facta narrantes. Aliter: Sicut in oculis apostoli, ita in genis illius Ecclesiae intelliguntur, quae ab apostolis in primordiis crediderunt, et ex sancta conversatione ad donum virtutum velut areola aromatum ex se perduxerunt, quae a piis scilicet praedicatoribus consitae, id est eruditae sunt, « Labia illius lilia distillantia myrrham primam. » Labia illius verba doctrinae. Labia, quia claritate coelestis regni promittunt. Distillantia myrrham primam, quia per contemptum voluptatum praesentium ad hanc perveniendum esse praedicant. Aliter: Labia 615DChristi doctores sunt illius, mente vel etiam carne virgines. Et in hoc passionem et mortem ipsius praedicantes, ut pro illo qui pro nobis mortuus est non recusemus suscipere mortem.

« Manus illius tornatiles aureae, » etc. Manus opera sunt illius, quia quae verbis docuit factis implevit. Tornatiles aureae, quia in se omnem regulam justitiae tenent, ut ipse dicit: « Sic enim decet nos implere omnem justitiam (Matth. III). » Aureae dicuntur, quia omnia quae in homine gessit, divinitatis gloria implevit. Plenae hyacinthis, quia ad spem nos coelestium atque amorem excitat, quia hyacinthus aetherei coloris gemma est. Possunt etiam in manibus Christi illi deputari, qui ea quae bona sunt operari 616Anon desinunt, quia ad omne opus bonum ob spem regni coelorum semper persistunt, et prompti sunt ad perficiendum.

« Venter illius eburneus, distinctus sapphiris. » Venter ergo dilecti fragilitatem humanitatis ejus designat. Ebur, decorem castitatis; sapphirus, sublimitatem virtutum coelestium. Distinctus sapphiris, quia partim humana fragilitas esurie, tentatione, fatigatione, morte: partim divina celsitudo miraculis, resurrectione et ascensionis gloria intelligitur. Possunt per ventrem hi in corpore Christi intelligi, qui credentes in fide bonum parturiunt opus. Intelligitur venter eburneus, fons sacri baptismatis, vel eburneus, propter perseverantiam castitatis, ex quo omnes credentes in fide nascuntur, et perseverant in castitate fidei et mentis.

616B« Crura illius columnae marmoreae, quae fundatae sunt super bases aureas. » Crurum vocabulo itinera incarnationis Christi insinuantur, quae columnis marmoreis propter firmitatem et rectitudinem comparantur. Quae fundatae sunt super bases aureas, quia quidquid per eum, vel de eo gestum est, omnia divinae promissionis consilio ante tempora saecularia disposita sunt. Aliter: Crura quae totum corpus bajulant, illi sunt qui donum Ecclesiae, velut donum columnae sustinent. Denique et in tabernaculo et in templo columnae argenteae fabricatae sunt, in significatione doctorum, qui fabricam Ecclesiae sustentant. Fundati super bases aureas, hoc est praecedentium patrum praedicationem, et per libros legis et Evangelii roborati. « Species ejus ut Libani. » 616CUt Libanus celsitudine et gratia arborum alios montes, sic nimirum Dominus Christus omnes sanctos meritorum celsitudine, et gratia virtutum antecellit. « Electus ut cedrus. » Sicut cedrus alias arbores quae in Libano nascuntur superat dignitate sua, ita Christus omnes qui in Ecclesia ad vitam nascuntur, sua transcendit gloria.

« Guttur illius suavissimum, et totus desiderabilis. » In gutture interna dulcedo verborum illius memoratur, quam qui sapit non esurit. Possunt et in gutture illi intelligi qui mysteria Christi exponunt, et in lege ipsius quotidie meditantur, qui verbum praedicationis ex ore depromunt. Et totus desiderabilis: O homo, quid amplius quaeris? Totus desiderabilis, quia totus Deus et totus homo est, in quem 616Ddesiderant angeli prospicere. Deus in majestate Patris, homo in virginitate matris: in illa Creator, in hac Salvator. « Talis est dilectus meus, et ipse est amicus meus, filiae Hierusalem. » Gratulando exsultans loquitur Ecclesia propter reconciliationem, et cognita mysteria sui sponsi, ac si gratulabunda dicat, et familiarius effecta: Quanto devotius quisque diligit Deum, tanto familiarius habet amicum eum, et talem necesse est, inquit, ut intelligas eum, si vis illum habere amicum tuum.

« Quo abiit dilectus tuus, o pulcherrima mulierum, quo declinavit dilectus tuus? Et quaeremus eum tecum. » Haec vox adolescentularum est quae 617Aloquuntur ad sponsam. Pro decore enim carminis variantur personae loquentium, sed tamen Christi Ecclesiam, quae vocabulo sponsae exprimitur, designat haec pulcherrima mulierum. Pulchrae sunt singulae sanctorum ecclesiae, sed pulcherrima est universitas totius sanctae Ecclesiae per totum orbem. Aliter: Recte enim sancta Ecclesia pulcherrima mulierum appellatur, quia virgo et incorrupta est, cui Apostolus loquens, ait: « Despondi enim vos uni viro virginem castam exhibere Christo (II Cor. XI). » Dicuntur itaque mulieres plebes haereticorum Judaeorum, hypocritarumque, sed corrupta fide et opere. Sed sola Ecclesia non solum fonte baptismatis et varietate virtutum pulcherrima est, sed fide et opere, non habens maculam aut rugam. Aiunt enim 617Badolescentulae: Quo declinavit dilectus tuus? quaeremus eum tecum, id est qui aliquando corporali specie in terra versatus est, dic quo declinavit ille, ut illum sequamur tecum.

CHAPTER V.

  • 612A

« Let my beloved come into his garden, and eat the fruit of his apple trees. » That is, let Christ come into his Church, and himself keep her everywhere unspotted, and make her fruitful with the fruit of faith, and gladly look upon and thankfully receive the works of his saints. « I am come into my garden, my sister, my bride. » This is not the voice of one commanding, but of one declaring a past time. As if he were to say: I have come, he says, most often into my Church, and I come to correct, to help and to confirm, even as thou hast besought. « I have gathered my myrrh with my aromatical spices. » By myrrh, suffering or mortification; by the aromatical spices, all the virtues are expressed. He reaps the myrrh with the aromatical spices, since he leads the martyrs, with the rest of the elect, to the ripeness 612Bof their rewards. « I have eaten the honeycomb with my honey, and I have drunk my wine with my milk. » In the honeycomb and the wine the preachers are understood, in the honey and the milk the hearers, and over both is the inward judge who proves and rewards. « Eat, O friends, and drink, and be inebriated, my dearly beloved. I sleep, and my heart watcheth. » It is the voice of Christ to the Church. As if he were to say openly: Friends, by doing the things I command. Dearly beloved, by embracing with whole charity. I beseech you therefore, fill your inward parts with the deeds of the saints, as with choice banquets. To whom the Church answered: « I sleep, and my heart watcheth, » that is: by the giving of God's grace, I worship him in the peace of the present life; and the more tranquilly I am free from inward assaults, the more loftily I see how good God is. Morally, a man sleeps with a watching heart 612Cwho, by the fact that he advances inwardly in contemplating, rests from restless work without. But among these things it is to be known that, so long as one lives in this mortal flesh, no man so advances in the virtue of contemplation that he fixes the eyes of his mind upon that ray of light which is now uncircumscribed. For neither is almighty God yet beheld in his own brightness, but the soul discerns something beneath that brightness, whereby, being refreshed, it may advance, and afterward attain to the glory of his vision. This voice can also be understood as addressed to the apostles: « Eat and drink. » As if he were to say: Let there be joy and refreshment to us from the conversion of the peoples, who pass over into your unity.

« The voice of my beloved knocking: Open to me, my sister, my love, my dove, my undefiled. » The Lord knocks when he teaches through 612Dholy doctrine the things that are good. Or he knocks at the door of our heart when he rouses us to the increase of the virtues. For the Church opens when she lays bare to him the inmost parts of her heart. She is called sister, because she is co-heir through the blood of Christ. Love, because she is privy to his secrets, or through love, because she keeps the things that are commanded. Dove, because she is enlightened by the gift of the Holy Spirit. Undefiled, because she alone is worthy of the sight of God. « For my head is full of dew, and the locks of my head of the drops of the nights. » The head of Christ is God. The locks are the gatherings of the faithful. In the dew and the drops of the nights is shown the charity growing cold in many, which they ought to have had toward God and toward their neighbors. Therefore of necessity the Lord rouses each of the faithful to the zeal 613Aof preaching. And to him, calling her to the labor of teaching, the Church answered:

« I have put off my garment, how shall I put it on? » As if she were to say openly: I have forsaken worldly business for thy sake, how shall I seek it again? « I have washed my feet, how shall I defile them? » That is: I have already washed away earthly thoughts with the tears of secret compunction, how shall I be polluted again with the filth of the world? Because the office of preaching can scarcely be without worldly occupation. Otherwise: The priests in the Church, who in the very congregation of the faithful minister the visible sacraments, are described as certain locks in her adornment. But because for the most part they are darkened by the village of this mortality, or by works of darkness, weighed down as with the drops of the nights, they seem as it were to cleave to Christ. 613BBut because they do not sincerely announce that same Christ, therefore, groaning for such men, there follows: « I have stripped off my garment, how shall I put it on? I have washed my feet, how shall I defile them? » That is: coming to the faith, I have already laid aside the old man according to my former manner of life, and have put on the new; and if any defilement came upon my feet while I was walking among earthly things, it has been washed away by Christ's washing; and now I behold the ministers of the sacraments, by whom I ought to be ordained, infected with carnal vices. Is it right that I should suffer myself again to be defiled by my former evils, by living ill after their examples?

« My beloved put his hand through the key hole. » My beloved indeed puts his hand through the key hole 613Cwhen the Lord invisibly kindles us by hidden compunction to the work of the virtues, and teaches us to pass through the strait and narrow way to the kingdoms of heaven, and calls back to our memory how and how greatly he suffered for us, coming down from the bosom of the Father. « And my belly trembled at his touch. » The bride, recalling these things in her inmost conscience, wholly terrified, accusing the sloth of her rest, hastens to the labor of preaching. Morally: The beloved puts his hand through the key hole when by his power the Lord knocks at our mind through a subtle understanding. And the belly trembles at his touch, because our weakness, by the very fact that it is touched by an understanding of heavenly joy, is troubled by its own exultation; and there comes to be in the mind a dread within the gladness, because it now feels what it loves of heavenly 613Djoy, and still fears that it may not obtain what it scarcely and faintly feels. What therefore remains, save what follows:

« I arose up to open to my beloved: my hands dropped with myrrh, and my fingers were full of the choicest myrrh. » It is the voice of the bride. To arise is to awaken in heart to Christ. To open is to preach the word of God, or to bring forth pure confusion. In the hands works are signified, in the fingers discretion, or else the martyrs. In the myrrh, the mortification of the flesh, or sufferings. Which is then the choicest, when either continence is practiced, or sufferings are undergone, solely for the charity of God and of one's neighbor

« I opened the bolt of my door to my beloved. » The Church 614Aopened the bolt to the Bridegroom, because she makes the temple of her breast worthy of the divine visitation and indwelling. « But he had turned aside and was gone. » Because to no one in this life is the full vision of God granted, as it is in the life to come, therefore he is said to pass by, because in the life to come he shows himself more fully to be seen and enjoyed. « My soul melted, as the beloved spoke. » The Church continues in the wonder of her amazement. The more sweetly, she says, I received the voice and the nearness of my beloved, the more loftily did whatever was cold in me grow warm with charity, and whatever was rigid melted away. « I sought him and found him not. » Because the gift of compunction and of inward sweetness is not in the choice of him who wills, but in the mercy of him who gives. Therefore they do not always have it in equal measure, because God does not so offer himself 614Bto those laboring in exile as to those reigning in the fatherland.

« The keepers that go about the city found me: they struck me and wounded me: the keepers of the walls took away my cloak from me. » The keepers of the city, that is, of the Church, are the holy teachers, who by diligent preaching go about the hearts of individuals, and wound them with the darts of heavenly love, and set them aflame that they may burn the more, and take from them the covering of their old manner of life. By the keepers of the city there can also be understood the powers of this world, who are known to rule the commonwealth as a kind of city. For these, seizing the elect Church in her first beginnings as she believed in Christ, laid her waste with blows and torments, and, slaying her priests and ministers, 614Ctook away, as it were, a certain cloak of her adornment.

« I adjure you, O daughters of Jerusalem, if you find my beloved, that you tell him that I languish with love. » Who the daughters of Jerusalem are has often been said: rightly does the bride languish when, struck with the sword of the word of God, she puts off the mantle of earthly action. And she adjures the daughters of Jerusalem, that is, the souls worthy of God, to carry the greatness of her love to God, and to ask for themselves heavenly help toward seeing his glory.

« What manner of one is thy beloved of the beloved, O thou most beautiful among women? what manner of one is thy beloved of the beloved, that thou hast so adjured us? » It is the voice of the holy preachers 614Dquestioning her and inquiring after the faith of the Church from her answer; that is: I beseech thee, since thou hast so adjured me, that thou show me the love by which thou sayest thou languishest, and make manifest what manner of one thy beloved is of the beloved, that is, the Son of the Father, God of God, and in what part he may be loved and not feared. since « perfect charity casteth out fear. »

« My beloved is white and ruddy, chosen out of thousands. » The beloved of the Church, Christ, is white, because without sin. Otherwise: He is called white according to that word, « in the beginning was the Word, and the Word was with God, and the Word was God. » Ruddy, because « the Word was made flesh and dwelt among us. » Or white in virginity, ruddy 615Ain the blood of his passion. Chosen out of thousands, because he alone is the mediator of God and men.

« His head is as the finest gold. » The head of Christ is God. As among metals nothing is better than gold, so neither among creatures shall anything be compared to the Creator, because he alone is good and best. « His locks are as branches of palm trees, black as a raven. » The locks of Christ are the companies of the saints, which cleave to God in faithful service. Branches of palm, according to that word of the Apostle: « Our conversation is in heaven (Phil. III): » Black, on account of their contempt among men, or because they testify that they are sinners. They can also be called branches on account of victory, and black on account of afflictions: The Bridegroom praised the beauty of the bride, and therefore the bride renders like for like and praises him.

615B« His eyes are as doves upon brooks of waters. » That is, the holy teachers, through whom the Church sees the things that are right: who are well compared to doves on account of simplicity and innocence, and to brooks of waters on account of the divine free gifts. « Which are washed with milk, and sit beside the plentiful streams. » He calls them washed with milk, that is, cleansed with most sweet grace, or purified through baptism. The plentiful streams are the abundance of all spiritual gifts. In the brooks there can be designated the instruction of the old law, and in the streams the fullest perfection of evangelical doctrine, because the holy preachers bring forth from their treasures new things and old (Matth. XIII).

« His cheeks are as beds of aromatical spices, set by the perfumers. » In the cheeks of our Saviour his modest 615Cmercy, and at the same time his severity, is expressed. By the beds of aromatical spices his virtues and his sweetness and the report of his glory are designated. Set by the perfumers, that is, by the prophets and the apostles, who tell the secrets of his incarnation to come, and his deeds. Otherwise: As in the eyes the apostles, so in his cheeks are understood those Churches which in the first beginnings believed through the apostles, and out of a holy manner of life brought forth from themselves, as it were a bed of aromatical spices, unto the gift of the virtues; which beds were set, that is, instructed, by devout preachers, that is to say, « His lips are lilies dropping choice myrrh. » His lips are the words of doctrine. Lips, because by their brightness they promise the heavenly kingdom. Dropping choice myrrh, because they preach that it is to be attained through the contempt of present pleasures. Otherwise: The lips 615Dof Christ are his teachers, virgins in mind or even in flesh. And in this they preach his passion and death, that for him who died for us we should not refuse to undergo death.

« His hands are turned and golden, » etc. His hands are his works, because what he taught in words he fulfilled in deeds. Turned and golden, because they hold in themselves the whole rule of justice, as he himself says: « For so it becometh us to fulfil all justice (Matth. III). » They are called golden, because all that he did in man, he filled with the glory of his divinity. Full of hyacinths, because he rouses us to the hope and the love of heavenly things, since the hyacinth is a gem of an ethereal color. There can also be reckoned among the hands of Christ those who do not cease to work the things that are good, 616Abecause for the hope of the kingdom of heaven they always persist in every good work, and are ready to bring it to perfection.

« His belly is of ivory, set with sapphires. » The belly, then, of the beloved designates the frailty of his humanity. The ivory, the comeliness of chastity; the sapphire, the loftiness of the heavenly virtues. Set with sapphires, because partly his human frailty is understood in hunger, temptation, weariness, death; partly his divine loftiness in miracles, in the resurrection and in the glory of the ascension. By the belly there can be understood those in the body of Christ who, believing, bring forth in faith a good work. The belly of ivory is also understood as the font of holy baptism, or of ivory on account of the perseverance of chastity, out of which all believers are born in faith and persevere in the chastity of faith and of mind.

616B« His legs are as pillars of marble, that are set upon bases of gold. » By the word "legs" the journeys of Christ's incarnation are intimated, which are compared to pillars of marble on account of their firmness and straightness. Which are set upon bases of gold, because whatsoever was done by him or concerning him, all of it was ordained before the ages of the world by the counsel of the divine promise. Otherwise: The legs, which bear the whole body, are they who uphold the gift of the Church, as it were the gift of the pillars. In fine, both in the tabernacle and in the temple pillars of silver were wrought, in signification of the teachers who uphold the fabric of the Church. Set upon bases of gold, that is, upon the preaching of the preceding fathers, and strengthened through the books of the law and of the Gospel. « His stature is as that of Libanus. » 616CAs Libanus excels the other mountains in loftiness and in the grace of its trees, so assuredly the Lord Christ excels all the saints in the loftiness of merits and in the grace of virtues. « Chosen as the cedar. » [Vulg. ut cedri, "as the cedars"] As the cedar surpasses in its own dignity the other trees that grow on Libanus, so Christ transcends by his own glory all who are born to life in the Church.

« His throat is most sweet, and he is all lovely. » In the throat the inward sweetness of his words is recorded, and he who tastes it does not hunger. In the throat there can also be understood those who expound the mysteries of Christ, and daily meditate on his law, who bring forth from their mouth the word of preaching. And he is all lovely: O man, what more dost thou seek? He is all lovely, because he is wholly God and wholly man, on whom 616Dthe angels desire to look. God in the majesty of the Father, man in the virginity of his mother: in the one the Creator, in the other the Saviour. « Such is my beloved, and he is my friend, O daughters of Jerusalem. » The Church speaks exulting with thanksgiving, on account of her reconciliation and the known mysteries of her Bridegroom, and, as it were rejoicing and made more familiar, she says: The more devoutly a man loves God, the more familiarly he holds him as a friend; and such, she says, must thou understand him to be, if thou wilt have him for thy friend.

« Whither is thy beloved gone, O thou most beautiful among women? whither is thy beloved turned aside? and we will seek him with thee. » This is the voice of the young maidens who 617Aspeak to the bride. For the persons of the speakers are varied for the comeliness of the song, but nevertheless this most beautiful among women designates the Church of Christ, which is expressed by the word "bride." Beautiful are the several churches of the saints, but most beautiful is the whole body of the entire holy Church throughout the whole world. Otherwise: For rightly is holy Church called most beautiful among women, because she is a virgin and uncorrupted, to whom the Apostle, speaking, says: « For I have espoused you to one husband, that I may present you as a chaste virgin to Christ (II Cor. XI). » And so the peoples of the heretics, of the Jews and of the hypocrites are called women, but corrupted in faith and in work. But the Church alone is most beautiful not only in the font of baptism and in the variety of the virtues, but in faith and in work, not having spot or wrinkle. For 617Bthe young maidens say: Whither is thy beloved turned aside? we will seek him with thee; that is: tell us whither he has turned aside who once dwelt on earth in bodily form, that we may follow him with thee.

CAPUT VI.

« Dilectus meus descendit in hortum suum ad areolam aromatum, » et reliqua. Quasi dixisset: Illuc ascendit, unde descendit. In hortum, id est Ecclesiam suam, ut eam fonte gratiae suae, quasi areolam aromatum irrigaret, ut virtutum floribus germinaret. Aliter: Dilectus Ecclesiae, Christus, descendit in hortum suum, quando visitavit priorem populum suum. Areolam aromatis ipsam sanctam Mariam virginem, de qua secundum carnem natus 617Cest, dicit: « Ut pascatur in hortis, et lilia colligat. » Id est, ut regnet in gentibus seu in Ecclesiis delectetur, ut ex eis virgines quasque vel sanctos assumat. Aliter: « Ut pascatur in hortis et lilia colligat. » Pascitur in hortis, dum piis sanctorum laboribus delectatur. Lilia colligit, dum ad perfectorum meritorum candorem pervenientes ad coelestia regna perducit.

« Ego dilecto meo, et dilectus meus mihi, qui pascitur inter lilia. » Haec loquitur Ecclesia ex fiducia amoris sui. Ego dilecto meo locum habitationis praeparo in me, et ipse mihi apud se. Ego regnum illius, et ipse rex meus. Ego corpus ejus, et ipse caput meum: qui semper inter sancta desideria castarum mentium, sive corporum pascitur, id est 617Ddelectatur. Hactenus sanctae Ecclesiae vox est quaerentis ac laudantis, nunc quid quaesitus respondeat, subinfertur.

« Pulchra es, amica mea, suavis et decora sicut Hierusalem, terribilis ut castrorum acies ordinata. » Pulchra es, sancta Ecclesia, per justitiam: amica, quia semper pacifica, quia enim Hierusalem pacis visio interpretatur, cujus nomine patria coelestis exprimitur. Sancta Ecclesia suavis et decora ut Hierusalem dicitur, quia ejus vita et desiderium visioni jam pacis intimae comparantur, ut in eo quod auctorem suum diligit, quod ejus speciem videre concupiscit, de quo scriptum est (I Petr. I): « In quem desiderant angeli prospicere, » per ipsa jam 618Aamoris sui desideria angelis similis dicatur. Quae quanto Deo amabilis efficitur, tanto agit ut malignis spiritibus terribilis fiat. Qualiter autem sit terribilis, subjuncta comparatione ostenditur, id est ut castrorum acies ordinata. Scimus et constat quia castrorum acies tunc hostibus terribilis est, quando ita fuerit constipata ac densata, ut in nullo loco interrupta videatur. Nam si ita disponitur, ut locus vacuus per quem possit ingredi dimittatur, profecto jam suis hostibus terribilis non est. Et nos ergo cum contra malignos spiritus spiritualis certaminis aciem ponimus, summopere necesse est ut per charitatem semper uniti atque constricti, et nunquam interrupti per discordiam inveniamur: quia quaelibet in nobis bona opera fuerint, si charitas desit? Per malum 618Bdiscordiae, locus aperitur in acie, unde ad feriendum nos valeat hostis intrare. Antiquus vero inimicus castitatem in nobis si sine charitate fuerit non timet, quia ipse nec carne premitur, ut in ejus luxuriam dissolvatur. Abstinentiam non timet, quia ipse cibo non utitur, qui necessitate corporis non urgetur. Distributiones terrenarum rerum non timet, si eisdem opera charitatis desint, quia divitiarum subsidiis nec ipse eget. Valde hoc in nobis charitatem veram, id est amorem humilem quem nobis vicissim impendimus timet. Et nimis concordiae nostrae invidet, quia hanc nos timemus in terra quam ipse nolens tenere amisit in coelo. Bene ergo dicitur terribilis, et reliqua: quia electorum multitudine eo maligni spiritus pertimescunt, quo eos per charitatis concordiam 618Cunitos contra se et conglobatos aspiciunt.

« Averte oculos tuos a me, quia ipsi me avolare fecerunt. » Id est oculis mentis tuae noli quaerere in tuae peregrinationis itinere me perfecte cognoscere, quod fieri non potest, quia quo intentius me agnoscere quaeris, eo certius me incomprehensibilem esse intelligis. Et ne quaeras in via praemium, quod tibi in patria reservatur. Aliter vero sentitur, ac si diceret: Nonne semper in corpore conspiciendum requiras, quem semper per fidem melius cernis? Idcirco etenim in coelum ascendi, ut semper tibi localis appaream. « Capilli tui sicut greges caprarum, quae ascenderunt de monte Galaad. » Qui versiculi prius expositi sunt, sed repetitio firmitatis 618Dindicium est. In capillis populi, in dentibus doctores, in gemellis fetibus praecepta charitatis intelliguntur.

« Sicut cortex mali Punici, sic genae tuae absque oculis tuis. » Genae Ecclesiae spirituales sunt patres, ut superius diximus, qui virtutibus sunt mirabiles et moribus venerabiles, et in Christi cruce gloriari non erubescentes. Et.haec magna sunt valde quae videntur in ea, sed multo majora quae non videntur, et in futurum reservantur. Unde Propheta: « Omnis gloria ejus filiae regis ab intus. (Psal. XLIV). »

« Sexaginta sunt reginae, et octoginta concubinae, et adolescentularum non est numerus » Reginae sunt quae amore sponsi, et coelestis praemii intuitu per praedicationem veritatis et fidei, 619Aet sacri baptismatis fontem, sobolem aeterno regi spiritualem generant. Senarius numerus ad perfectionem praesentis vitae pertinet, propter sex dies in quibus licet bona operari. Concubinae vero simplices in Ecclesia significant, qui suscepto verbi semine, non spiritaliter, sed carnaliter sapiunt, sive qui carnali solummodo quodammodo Christum praedicando vel baptizando docent. Adolescentulae sunt animae nuper in Christo renatae, quarum summa propter multitudinem civium coelestium numerum transcendit. Possunt itaque in adolescentulis illi intelligi, qui semper quae sancta sunt audire cupiunt, non tamen audita operibus implere satagunt.

« Una est columba mea, perfecta mea; una est matri suae, electa genitricis suae. » Sancta videlicet 619BEcclesia universalis per totum orbem una est matri suae, id est coelesti Jerusalem, quae est mater omnium nostrum, unde ad nos gratia Spiritus sancti descendit per quam renascimur Deo. Una vero dicitur, quia, expulsis cunctis reprobis, ipsa tantummodo sociabitur Christo. « Viderunt eam filiae Sion et beatissimam praedicaverunt reginae, et concubinae laudaverunt eam. » Quas prius dixit adolescentulas, nunc filias nominat. Reginae et concubinae idem sunt quae et ante, quae omnes catholicam laudant Ecclesiam.

« Quae est ista quae ascendit quasi aurora consurgens, pulchra ut luna, electa ut sol, terribilis ut castrorum acies ordinata? » Vox est ista Synagogae admirantis pulchritudinem Ecclesiae ex gentibus, 619Ceo quod sine judiciis legis et instructione prophetarum ad praedicationem Evangelii tam subito tanta perfectione virtutum excreverit, sicut superius admirata jam fuerat. Ait namque.: « Quae est ista quae ascendit sicut aurora consurgens, » etc. Progreditur Ecclesia quasi aurora, quia jam veri luminis in ea sol ortus mundo post tenebras longae ignorantiae monstratur, vel quando relinquens post se tenebrarum opera caste et sobrie vivit, et ad perfectum diem tendit: « Pulchra ut luna, » quia a sole justitiae illustrata, noctem mundi illuminat. « Electa ut sol, » quia imaginem sui Conditoris in omni justitiae sanctitate et veritate portat. Aliter: « Pulchra ut luna, » per virtutem in praesenti saeculo et decorem. « Electa ut sol, » in futura beatitudine. Vel « pulchra 619Dut luna, » in conjugatis: « electa ut sol, » in virginibus: terribilis aereis potestatibus, et per potestatem ligandi et solvendi. Ordinata in unitate fidei, spei, et charitatis, et in mysteriorum dispositione.

« Descendi in hortum nucum, ut viderem poma convallium. » Hortus etenim nucum est Ecclesia praesens, ubi nostras conscientias alterutrum minime videmus, sed fracta per tentationem testa carnis vel corporis, apparebit internae dulcedinis gustus. Poma convallis, fructus humilitatis: descendit enim sancta Ecclesia per doctores sanctos, ut videat qui proficiant ad fructus bonorum, quive adhuc indigeant doctrinae irrigatione. Potest et hortus nucum canon Novi ac Veteris Testamenti designari: in quo 620Asub tegmine litterae velut sub cortice spiritualis latet sensus, « Ut inspicerem si floruissent vineae. » Id est, si plebs fidelis in studio virtutum floruisset. « Et germinassent mala Punica. » Si qui ad exemplum Dominicae passionis praeparati essent suum fundere sanguinem.

« Nescivi: anima mea conturbavit me propter quadrigas Amminadab. » Vox Synagogae: Nescivi dona gratiae spiritualis in te esse sponsa, sed anima mea conturbavit me, propter inductionem Novi Testamenti et Evangelicae quadrigae. Amminadab quippe interpretatur populi mei spontaneus, id est Christus, qui per totum vehitur orbem. Cui Ecclesia consolando mox respondit:

« Revertere, revertere, Sunamitis; revertere, revertere, 620But intueamur te. » Id est, Noli turbata esse, sed revertere ad cognitionem tui Redemptoris, qui tibi potius in prophetis et lege promissus est. Revertere puritate fidei, revertere operum perfectione. O Sunamitis, id est captiva, jam a vinculo infidelitatis revertere ad tuum Redemptorem, ut salveris!

CHAPTER VI.

« My beloved is gone down into his garden, to the little bed of spices, » and the rest. As though he had said: Thither he ascended, whence he came down. Into the garden, that is, into his Church, that he might water her with the fountain of his grace, as it were a little bed of spices, that she might bud forth with the flowers of the virtues. Otherwise: The beloved of the Church, Christ, came down into his garden when he visited his former people. By the little bed of spice he means holy Mary the Virgin herself, of whom he was born 617Caccording to the flesh: « That he may feed in the gardens, and gather lilies. » That is, that he may reign among the nations, or take his delight in the Churches, that out of them he may take to himself virgins and saints. Otherwise: « That he may feed in the gardens and gather lilies. » He feeds in the gardens while he takes delight in the holy labors of the saints. He gathers lilies while he leads to the heavenly kingdoms those who come to the whiteness of perfect merits.

« I to my beloved, and my beloved to me, who feedeth among the lilies. » These things holy Church speaks out of the confidence of her love. I prepare for my beloved a place of dwelling in myself, and he for me with himself. I am his kingdom, and he my king. I am his body, and he my head: who is ever fed, that is 617Ddelighted, among the holy desires of chaste minds or bodies. Thus far it is the voice of holy Church seeking and praising; now what he who is sought may answer is subjoined.

« Thou art beautiful, O my love, sweet and comely as Jerusalem, terrible as an army set in array. » Thou art beautiful, holy Church, through justice: my love, because ever peaceable; for Jerusalem is interpreted the vision of peace, by whose name the heavenly fatherland is expressed. Holy Church is called sweet and comely as Jerusalem, because her life and her desire are already compared to the vision of inmost peace, so that in this — that she loves her author, that she longs to see his beauty, of whom it is written (I Petr. I): « Upon whom the angels desire to look, » — she may already, 618Aby the very desires of her love, be called like to the angels. And the more she is made lovable to God, the more she brings it about that she becomes terrible to the malignant spirits. But in what manner she is terrible is shown by the comparison subjoined, that is, as an army set in array. We know, and it is agreed, that an army is then terrible to its enemies, when it has been so packed close and thickened that in no place does it seem broken. For if it is so disposed that an empty place is left through which one might enter, assuredly it is no longer terrible to its enemies. We too therefore, when we set the line of spiritual combat against the malignant spirits, it is above all needful that we be found always united and bound together through charity, and never broken through discord: for whatever good works there shall have been in us, if charity be wanting? Through the evil 618Bof discord a place is opened in the line, whence the enemy may be able to enter to strike us. But the ancient enemy does not fear chastity in us if it be without charity, because he himself is not pressed by flesh, that he should be dissolved into its wantonness. He does not fear abstinence, because he himself uses no food, being one who is not urged by the necessity of the body. He does not fear the distributions of earthly goods, if to those same the works of charity be wanting, because neither does he himself need the supports of riches. This he greatly fears in us — true charity, that is the humble love which we bestow upon one another in turn. And he exceedingly envies our concord, because we fear this on earth which he himself, unwilling to hold it, lost in heaven. Well therefore is she called terrible, and the rest: because the malignant spirits are the more affrighted at the multitude of the elect, in that they behold them, united through the concord of charity, 618Cmassed together against themselves.

« Turn away thy eyes from me, for they have made me flee away. » That is: with the eyes of thy mind do not seek to know me perfectly on the journey of thy pilgrimage, which cannot be done, because the more intently thou seekest to know me, the more certainly thou understandest me to be incomprehensible. And do not seek on the way the reward which is reserved for thee in the fatherland. But it is understood otherwise, as if he said: Wouldst thou not always seek him to be beheld in the body, whom thou always discernest better through faith? For this cause indeed I ascended into heaven, that I might always appear to thee as bound to a place. « Thy hair is as flocks of goats, which have come up from mount Galaad. » [Vulg. at Cant. 6:4 grex caprarum quae apparuerunt de Galaad, "a flock of goats that have appeared from Galaad"; the wording printed here is that of Cant. 4:1] These little verses have been expounded before, but repetition is a token 618Dof steadfastness. In the hairs are understood the peoples, in the teeth the doctors, in the twin young the precepts of charity.

« As the rind of a pomegranate, so are thy cheeks, besides thy eyes. » [Vulg. absque occultis tuis, "besides that which is hidden within thee"] The cheeks of the Church are the spiritual fathers, as we have said above, who are wonderful in virtues and venerable in character, and not ashamed to glory in the cross of Christ. And these things Et.haec which are seen in her are exceedingly great, but far greater are those which are not seen, and are reserved for the time to come. Whence the Prophet: « All the glory of the king's daughter is within. (Psal. XLIV). »

« There are sixty queens, and eighty concubines, and of young maidens there is no number » The queens are they who, out of love of the bridegroom and with a view to the heavenly reward, beget spiritual offspring for the eternal king through the preaching of truth and of faith, 619Aand through the font of holy baptism. The number six pertains to the perfection of the present life, on account of the six days in which it is lawful to work good things. But the concubines signify the simple in the Church, who, having received the seed of the word, are wise not spiritually but carnally; or those who teach Christ, in a merely carnal manner as it were, by preaching or by baptizing. The young maidens are souls newly born again in Christ, whose sum, because of the multitude of the heavenly citizens, transcends number. And so by the young maidens may be understood those who always desire to hear the things that are holy, yet do not busy themselves to fulfil in works the things they have heard.

« One is my dove, my perfect one; she is one to her mother, the chosen of her that bore her. » [Vulg. una est matris suae, electa genetrici suae, "she is the one of her mother, the chosen one to her that bore her" — the cases are inverted here] Holy 619BChurch, namely, the universal Church throughout the whole world, is one to her mother, that is to the heavenly Jerusalem, which is the mother of us all, whence the grace of the Holy Spirit descends to us, through which we are born again to God. And she is called one because, when all the reprobate have been cast out, she alone shall be joined to Christ. « The daughters of Sion saw her, and declared her most blessed; the queens and the concubines praised her. » [Vulg. Viderunt eam filiae, without Sion] Those whom he before called young maidens he now names daughters. The queens and the concubines are the same as before, and all of them praise the Catholic Church.

« Who is she that cometh up as the morning rising, fair as the moon, chosen as the sun, terrible as an army set in array? » [Vulg. quae progreditur, "that goeth forward" — the word the gloss below itself uses] This is the voice of the Synagogue admiring the beauty of the Church out of the nations, 619Cin that, without the judgments of the law and the instruction of the prophets, she had grown up at the preaching of the Gospel so suddenly and with so great a perfection of virtues, even as she had already marvelled above. For she says.: « Who is she that cometh up as the morning rising, » etc. The Church goes forward as the dawn, because now the sun of the true light is shown to have risen in her upon the world after the darkness of long ignorance; or when, leaving behind her the works of darkness, she lives chastely and soberly, and tends toward the perfect day: « Fair as the moon, » because, illumined by the sun of justice, she lights up the night of the world. « Chosen as the sun, » because she bears the image of her Creator in all justice, holiness, and truth. Otherwise: « Fair as the moon, » through virtue and comeliness in the present world. « Chosen as the sun, » in the blessedness to come. Or « fair 619Das the moon, » in the married; « chosen as the sun, » in virgins: terrible to the powers of the air, and through the power of binding and loosing. Set in array in the unity of faith, of hope, and of charity, and in the disposition of the mysteries.

« I went down into the garden of nuts, to see the fruits of the valleys. » For the garden of nuts is the present Church, where we in no wise see one another's consciences; but when the shell of the flesh or of the body has been broken through temptation, the taste of the inward sweetness will appear. The fruits of the valley are the fruit of humility: for holy Church goes down through the holy doctors, that she may see who advance to the fruits of good things, and who as yet need the watering of doctrine. The garden of nuts may also be taken to designate the canon of the New and the Old Testament: in which, 620Aunder the covering of the letter, as under a rind, the spiritual sense lies hid. « That I might see if the vines had flourished. » That is, if the faithful people had flourished in the pursuit of the virtues. « And if the pomegranates had budded. » If any had been made ready, after the example of the Lord's passion, to shed their own blood.

« I knew not: my soul troubled me for the chariots of Amminadab. » The voice of the Synagogue: I knew not that the gifts of spiritual grace were in thee, O bride; but my soul troubled me, because of the bringing in of the New Testament and of the evangelical chariot. For Amminadab is interpreted the willing one of my people, that is Christ, who is borne through the whole world. To whom the Church, comforting her, forthwith answered:

« Return, return, O Sunamitess; return, return, 620Bthat we may behold thee. » [Vulg. Sulamitis] That is: Be not troubled, but return to the knowledge of thy Redeemer, who was rather promised to thee in the prophets and in the law. Return by purity of faith, return by perfection of works. O Sunamitess, that is, captive, return now from the bond of unbelief to thy Redeemer, that thou mayest be saved!

CAPUT VII.

« Quid videbis in Sunamite, nisi castrorum choros? » Hoc est: Tu doles Synagogam obduratam, prope est tempus quod choros bellantium adversus malignos spiritus, et laudantium Deum in ea videbis. Potest et aliter intelligi nescivi. Anima mea conturbavit me, propter quadrigas Amminadab. Quid nescivit Christus, nisi peccatum? Nescivit 620Cillos etiam quibus in judicio dicturus est: « Amen, dico vobis, nescio vos (Matth. XXV). » Quadrigas Amminadab, evangelistas dicit. Amminadab quippe interpretatur populi spontaneus, id est plebs Ecclesiae fidelis. Sunamitis ista Ecclesiam significat, cujus figuram Sunamitis illa in libro Regum gestavit. Qui ob hoc qui Christi sanguine decoratur, coccinea in Latino sonat cui frequenter a sanctis praedicatoribus dicitur « Revertere: » ut etiamsi per adversa deliquerit, in desperationem non decidat, sed a peccandi consuetudine cum spe indulgentiae resipiscat. « Quam pulchri sunt gressus tui in calceamentis, filia principis! » Nunc laudes Ecclesiae ab ipso sponso proferuntur, et primo operum constantia, et mortificatio voluptatum laudantur in ea quam et filiam 620Dprincipis, id est Christi, ob gratiam baptismatis et nobilitatem virtutum nominat. « Juncturae femorum tuorum sicut monilia quae fabricata sunt manu artificis. » Duorum concordia populorum speciali prole fecundantium, in junctura femorum designatur, quae sicut monilia fabricata sunt manu artificis, id est ineffabili largitate Conditoris nostri: in monilibus bona opera exprimuntur.

« Umbilicus tuus sicut crater tornatilis, nunquam indigens poculis. » Umbilicus tuus fragilitas nostrae infirmitatis. Crater fit tornatilis, cum conscientia mortalitatis nostrae admoniti, calicem salutaris verbi prompta mente propinare satagimus. Item in umbilico intelliguntur qui non extremo, sed in medio 621AEcclesiae corpore commorantur. In his ergo reperiuntur quae sunt vasa utilia: in eo tornatilis, quia ad obediendum vel ad omne opus bonum semper parati. In eo nunquam indigens poculis, quantum sancti Spiritus gratia ex toto repleti. « Venter tuus sicut acervus tritici vallatus liliis. » Hi namque qui semper sanctarum Scripturarum alimenta percipiunt, et ea quae sunt salutaria infra se congerunt, atque aliis doctrinam suam velut triticum erogantes praedicare non desinunt, in ventri similitudinem deputantur. Acervus tritici vallatus liliis est, cum abundantia boni operis sola perpetuae lucis spe colligitur. In ventre memoria, in tritico multiplicatio boni operis, in liliis castitas spei exprimitur.

621B« Duo ubera tua, sicut duo hinnuli gemelli capreae. » De hoc versu dictum est superius, sed repetitio firmitatis indicium est. Duo ubera doctores sunt utriusque populi. Hinnuli gemelli propter unam concordiam: item duo ubera Ecclesiae duo sunt Testamenta duobus populis ex traduce peccati venientibus coaptata. Hi enim tanquam hinnuli in Christo renascuntur atque ex his duobus Testamentis lac sapientiae sugentes.

« Collum tuum sicut turris eburnea. » In collo doctores designantur, qui turri eburneae propter firmitatem et pulchritudinem comparantur, quia civitati Dei robur praestant et decus. Potest et in collo notitia sacrarum Scripturarum designari, quae idcirco turris eburnea appellatur, quia qui verbo Dei insistunt, 621Cquotidiano profectu per nitorem justitiae in excelsiora proficiunt. « Oculi tui sicut piscinae in Hesebon, quae sunt in porta filiae multitudinis. » Oculi Ecclesiae doctores sunt propter providentiam, sicut collum propter nutrimentum. Et recte piscinae comparantur, quia fluenta doctrinae suis auditoribus praebere non cessant. Hesebon cingulum moeroris interpretatur, quia sancti pro vana laetitia carnis, cingulo abstinentiae constringuntur. Et bene in portis filiae multitudinis, ob abundantiam populorum concurrentium per portam fidei in Ecclesiam. Potest et per portam Christus intelligi. « Nasus tuus sicut turris Libani. » In naso verbi Dei dispensatores, causa discretionis designantur, qui odorem justitiae qui est in Christo percipientes, suis porrigunt auditoribus, atque intellectu 621Ddiscretionis inter odorem virtutum et vitiorum fetores ex alto discernunt. Turri assimilantur, qui per sapientiam eminentissimum locum tenent in Ecclesia. « Quae respicit contra Damascum, » id est sanguinarios et impios, quia Damascus sanguinem bibens interpretatur, significat carnales et crudeles, contra quos sancti doctores in turri Libani, id est firmitate Ecclesiae semper vigilant.

« Caput tuum ut Carmelus, et comae capitis tui sicut purpura regis vincta canalibus. » In capite mens, in comis cogitationes designantur: et sicut capite membra, ita mente cogitationes reguntur, ut in Carmelo, id est sublimitate virtutum et passione Christi quae nomine purpurae exprimitur, versentur. 622ACanales praecordia sunt sanctorum, in quibus alligantur tales cogitationes. Aliter: Caput Ecclesiae Christus, quo sublimius nihil est. Coma capitis eminentiores quique sancti, beati scilicet martyres, consimilati purpura, regalibus ornamentis, et salutaribus copulati doctrinis.

« Quam pulchra es et decora, charissima in deliciis. » Pulchra dicitur Ecclesia, fide et opere. Decora ornamentis virtutum. Charissima per dilectionem. « In deliciis, » id est in spiritualibus virtutibus.

« Statura tua assimilata est palmae, et ubera tua botris. » Id est rectitudo bonae operationis, semper ad victoriam tendit. Uberibus doctores Ecclesiae, propter lac primae eruditionis comparantur, et botris aequantur, propter mysteria dulcissimae aeternitatis.

622B« Dixi: Ascendam in palmam, apprehendam fructus ejus. » Apte enim victoriosissima crux palmae comparatur, in quam Christus ascendens apprehendit fructum ejus. Illi enim in palmam ascendunt qui in cruce Christi proficiunt. De quo Apostolus ait: « Qui enim sunt Christi, carnem suam crucifixerunt cum vitiis et concupiscentiis (Gal. V). » -- « Et erunt ubera tua sicut botri vineae, » id est dona quae largitus est sanctae Ecclesiae, quae ex illo tempore botros vineae germinavit, id est sanctos doctores, qui majore scientia et gratia post crucem et resurrectionem Salvatoris abundabant. Possunt et ubera praedicatores Ecclesiae qui renascentes in fide nutriunt, intelligi. « Et odor oris tui sicut odor malorum. 622CGuttur tuum sicut vinum optimum ad potandum. » In gutture vox praesentis doctrinae, in odore fama absentis designatur. Et ideo vino illa propter flagrantiam virtutum, haec malis, propter suavitatem absentis famae, comparantur: « Dignum dilecto meo. » Rapuit enim sponsa verbum ex ore sponsi, quia ille vino optimo eam comparavit, et subjecit. Dignum dilecto meo, id est tanta est sublimitas evangelicae praedicationis, ut ipse dilectus meus primus per incarnationem apparens, mundo iter coeleste aperuit: possunt in gutture illi intelligi qui per evangelicam praedicationem corda auditorum inebriant: unde Christus potatur, cum ex ea Christiani, qui membra ipsius sunt, incomparabili dulcedine satiantur. « Labiisque 622Det dentibus illius ad ruminandum, » id est apostolis et praedicatoribus quibus maxime dedit ad meditandum.

« Ego dilecto meo, » et non alteri cui totam curam servitutis et dilectionis impendo. « Et ad me conversio ejus, » id est, me solam diligit, et adjuvat me, ne deficiam in via. Cui dilecto ex voce Ecclesiae per Psalmistam dicitur: « Adhaesit anima mea post te, me suscepit dextera tua (Psal. LXII). »

« Veni, dilecte mi, egrediamur in agrum, commoremur in villis; mane surgamus ad vineas, videamus si floruit vinea, si flores fructus parturiunt, si floruerunt mala Punica. » Quia nullatenus Ecclesia, vel ad bonum operandum egrediendo procedere, vel in exercitio bonae operationis persistendo 623Acommorari, vel saltem ad propositum bene agendi assurgere, vel animos auditorum suorum quantum profecerint discernere sufficit, nisi Dei gratia adjuta qui dixit: « Ecce ego vobiscum sum omnibus diebus, usque ad consummationem saeculi (Matth. XXVIII). » Egreditur Christus cum Ecclesia in agrum, quando per sanctos doctores visitat mundum. Commoratur in villis, per fidem scilicet in populis ex gentibus credentibus, circumquaque diffusis. Mane consurgit ad vineas, ut animae quae per fidem jam meruerunt colligi, in matutinis Christum resurrexisse cognoscant, nec ultra de sui corporis resurrectione dubitent. Videamus si floruisset vinea, si Ecclesia flores fide et parturiat, si flores fructus parturiunt, id est si sancti fideles quod corde crediderunt ore 623Bpronuntiant. Si floruerunt mala Punica, si illi qui in Christo crediderunt in tanta dilectione proficiunt, ut etiam pro ipso occidi delectentur. His enim singulis sponsa dilecti sui poenitentiam quaerit. « Ibi tibi dabo ubera mea, » id est parvulorum meorum paedagogos. Quia in his omnibus proficiunt sancti doctores, in hac sancta confessione sociabuntur tibi doctores mei. Et quia parati sunt ut patiantur pro te, regnabunt apud te.

« Mandragorae dederunt odorem suum in portis nostris. » Mandragorae duo sunt genera, masculus et femina. Femina rubea, masculus albus: utrisque una vis ad medicamenta. Mandragorae dictae, eo quod habeant mala suaveolentia, in magnitudinem mali, unde et eam Latini malum terrae vocant: hanc 623Cpoetae ἀνθροπόμορφον appellant, quod habeat radicem formam hominis assimilantem; quae datur in vino ad bibendum, quorum corpus propter curam secandum est, ut soporati dolorem non sentiant. Mandragorae propter multimoda medicaminum genera sanctorum virtutibus comparantur, quae more medicorum ad salutem animarum a vitiorum infirmitatibus sanentur, ostenduntur. Portae Ecclesiae doctores sunt sancti, et in hujusmodi portis mandragorae dant odorem, cum spirituales quique ex se virtutum palmam longe lateque spargunt. « Omnia poma, nova et vetera, dilecte mi, servavi tibi. » Poma nova et vetera, praecepta sive promissa sunt Novi Testamenti et Veteris, quae omnia ad ejus gratiam refert Ecclesia, sive Patres Novi Testamenti et Veteris possunt 623Dintelligi, quia una eademque Ecclesia est.

CHAPTER VII.

« What shalt thou see in the Sunamitess, but the companies of the camps? » [Vulg. in Sulamite, nisi choros castrorum] That is: Thou grievest that the Synagogue is hardened; the time is at hand when thou shalt see in her the companies of those who war against the malignant spirits and who praise God. I knew not may also be understood otherwise. My soul troubled me, because of the chariots of Amminadab. What did Christ not know, save sin? He knew not 620Cthose also to whom he will say in the judgment: « Amen, I say to you, I know you not (Matth. XXV). » By the chariots of Amminadab he means the evangelists. For Amminadab is interpreted the willing one of the people, that is, the faithful people of the Church. This Sunamitess signifies the Church, whose figure that Sunamitess in the book of Kings bore. Which for this cause, which is adorned with the blood of Christ, sounds in the Latin scarlet; to whom it is frequently said by the holy preachers, « Return: » that even if through adversities she has fallen away, she may not sink down into despair, but may come to her senses out of the habit of sinning, with hope of pardon. « How beautiful are thy steps in shoes, O prince's daughter! » Now the praises of the Church are set forth by the bridegroom himself, and first the constancy of her works and the mortification of pleasures are praised in her, whom also he names the daughter 620Dof the prince, that is of Christ, on account of the grace of baptism and the nobility of her virtues. « The joints of thy thighs are like jewels, that are wrought by the hand of a craftsman. » The concord of the two peoples, made fruitful with a special offspring, is designated in the joint of the thighs, which are as jewels wrought by the hand of a craftsman, that is by the unspeakable bounty of our Creator: in the jewels good works are expressed.

« Thy navel is like a turned bowl, never wanting cups. » Thy navel is the frailty of our infirmity. The bowl is made turned when, admonished by the consciousness of our mortality, we busy ourselves to hand round with a ready mind the cup of the word of salvation. Likewise in the navel are understood those who dwell not at the edge but in the midst 621Aof the body of the Church. In these therefore are found the things that are useful vessels: in this it is turned, because they are always ready for obedience and for every good work. In this it never wants cups, inasmuch as they are wholly filled with the grace of the Holy Spirit. « Thy belly is like a heap of wheat, set about with lilies. » For those who always receive the nourishments of the Holy Scriptures, and gather within themselves the things that are salutary, and cease not to preach, dealing out their teaching to others like wheat, are reckoned in the likeness of the belly. It is a heap of wheat set about with lilies when the abundance of good work is gathered by the hope of perpetual light alone. In the belly memory is expressed, in the wheat the multiplication of good work, in the lilies the chastity of hope.

621B« Thy two breasts are like two twin fawns of a roe. » Of this verse it was spoken above, but repetition is a token of steadfastness. The two breasts are the doctors of either people. They are twin fawns because of their one concord: likewise the two breasts of the Church are the two Testaments, fitted to the two peoples that come from the propagation of sin. For these are born again in Christ as fawns, and sucking from these two Testaments the milk of wisdom.

« Thy neck is as a tower of ivory. » In the neck the doctors are designated, who are compared to a tower of ivory because of their firmness and their beauty, since they afford strength and comeliness to the city of God. In the neck may also be designated the knowledge of the Holy Scriptures, which is therefore called a tower of ivory, because those who apply themselves to the word of God, 621Cby daily progress advance to loftier things through the brightness of justice. « Thy eyes like the fishpools in Hesebon, which are in the gate of the daughter of the multitude. » The eyes of the Church are the doctors, because of their foresight, as the neck is because of nourishment. And rightly are they compared to fishpools, because they cease not to offer the streams of doctrine to their hearers. Hesebon is interpreted the girdle of sorrow, because the saints, in place of the vain gladness of the flesh, are bound with the girdle of abstinence. And well, in the gates of the daughter of the multitude, because of the abundance of the peoples running together through the gate of faith into the Church. By the gate Christ also may be understood. « Thy nose is as the tower of Libanus. » In the nose are designated the dispensers of the word of God, by reason of discernment, who, perceiving the odor of the justice which is in Christ, hold it out to their hearers, and by the understanding 621Dof discernment distinguish from on high between the odor of the virtues and the stenches of the vices. They are likened to a tower, who through wisdom hold the most eminent place in the Church. « Which looketh toward Damascus, » that is, toward the bloody and the impious, for Damascus is interpreted drinking blood, and signifies the carnal and the cruel, against whom the holy doctors keep watch always in the tower of Libanus, that is in the firmness of the Church.

« Thy head is like Carmel, and the hairs of thy head as the purple of the king bound in the channels. » In the head the mind is designated, in the hairs the thoughts: and as the members are ruled by the head, so are the thoughts by the mind, that they may be occupied in Carmel, that is in the loftiness of the virtues and in the passion of Christ, which is expressed by the name of purple. 622AThe channels are the inward parts of the saints, in which such thoughts are bound. Otherwise: The head of the Church is Christ, than whom nothing is more lofty. The hair of the head is all the more eminent saints, namely the blessed martyrs, made like to purple, joined to royal ornaments and to saving doctrines.

« How beautiful art thou, and comely, my dearest, in delights. » The Church is called beautiful, in faith and in work. Comely, by the ornaments of the virtues. Dearest, through love. « In delights, » that is, in spiritual virtues.

« Thy stature is likened to a palm tree, and thy breasts to clusters of grapes. » That is, the uprightness of good working ever tends to victory. To the breasts are compared the doctors of the Church, because of the milk of first instruction, and they are matched with clusters, because of the mysteries of most sweet eternity.

622B« I said: I will go up into the palm tree, I will take hold of the fruits thereof. » For fittingly is the most victorious cross compared to a palm, into which Christ, going up, took hold of its fruit. For they go up into the palm who advance in the cross of Christ. Whereof the Apostle says: « For they that are Christ's have crucified their own flesh with the vices and concupiscences (Gal. V). » -- « And thy breasts shall be as the clusters of the vine, » that is, the gifts which he bestowed upon holy Church, which from that time budded forth the clusters of the vine, that is the holy doctors, who after the cross and resurrection of the Savior abounded in greater knowledge and grace. By the breasts may also be understood the preachers of the Church, who nourish those that are born again in the faith. « And the odor of thy mouth like the odor of apples. 622CThy throat like the best wine to drink. » In the throat is designated the voice of teaching present, in the odor the fame of it absent. And therefore the one is compared to wine, because of the fragrance of the virtues, the other to apples, because of the sweetness of absent fame: « Worthy for my beloved. » For the bride snatched the word out of the mouth of the bridegroom, because he had compared her to the best wine, and she subjoined it. Worthy for my beloved, that is: so great is the loftiness of the evangelical preaching, that my beloved himself, first appearing through the incarnation, opened the heavenly way to the world: in the throat may be understood those who through evangelical preaching inebriate the hearts of the hearers: whence Christ is drunk, when out of her the Christians, who are his members, are filled with incomparable sweetness. « And for his lips 622Dand teeth to chew upon, » that is, for the apostles and the preachers, to whom above all he gave it to meditate upon.

« I to my beloved, » and to no other, upon whom I bestow the whole care of my service and of my love. « And his turning is toward me, » that is, he loves me alone, and helps me, that I fail not in the way. To which beloved it is said by the Psalmist in the voice of the Church: « My soul hath cleaved after thee: thy right hand hath received me (Psal. LXII). »

« Come, my beloved, let us go forth into the field, let us abide in the villages; let us get up early to the vineyards, let us see if the vineyard hath flourished, if the flowers bring forth fruits, if the pomegranates have flourished. » Since the Church is in no wise sufficient either to go forth and advance to the working of good, or to abide by persisting in the exercise of good working, 623Aor even to rise up to the purpose of acting well, or to discern how far the minds of her hearers have advanced, unless she be helped by the grace of God, who said: « Behold I am with you all days, even to the consummation of the world (Matth. XXVIII). » Christ goes forth with the Church into the field, when through the holy doctors he visits the world. He abides in the villages, namely through faith, among the peoples believing out of the nations, spread abroad on every side. He rises early to the vineyards, that the souls which through faith have already deserved to be gathered may know that Christ rose again in the morning hours, and may no longer doubt of the resurrection of their own body. Let us see if the vineyard had flourished, if the Church also brings forth flowers by faith, if the flowers bring forth fruits, that is, if the holy faithful pronounce with the mouth 623Bwhat they have believed with the heart. If the pomegranates have flourished, if they who have believed in Christ so advance in love that they even delight to be slain for him. For in each one of these the bride seeks the penitence of her beloved. « There will I give thee my breasts, » that is, the tutors of my little ones. Because in all these things the holy doctors advance, in this holy confession my doctors shall be joined to thee. And because they are ready to suffer for thee, they shall reign with thee.

« The mandrakes have given their odor in our gates. » [Vulg. Mandragorae dederunt odorem, without suum; there in portis nostris opens the clause that follows] Of mandrakes there are two kinds, the male and the female. The female is red, the male white: in both there is one power for medicines. They are called mandrakes because they have sweet-smelling apples, of the size of an apple, whence the Latins also call it the apple of the earth: this 623Cthe poets call ἀνθροπόμορφον, because it has a root resembling the form of a man; which is given in wine to drink to those whose body must be cut for the sake of treatment, that being lulled asleep they may not feel the pain. Mandrakes, because of their manifold kinds of medicines, are compared to the virtues of the saints, which, after the manner of physicians, are shown to be healed from the infirmities of the vices unto the health of souls. The gates of the Church are the holy doctors, and in gates of this sort the mandrakes give their odor, when all spiritual men spread abroad from themselves far and wide the palm of the virtues. « All the fruits, new and old, my beloved, I have kept for thee. » The new and old fruits are the precepts or the promises of the New and of the Old Testament, all of which the Church refers to his grace; or the Fathers of the New and of the Old Testament may 623Dbe understood, because the Church is one and the same.

CAPUT VIII.

« Quis mihi dat fratrem meum sugentem ubera mea? » Vox ista est antiquorum virorum optantium adventum Christi in carne et illum in Synagoga nasci ac nutriri, juxta humanae conditionis naturam. « Ut inveniam te foris et deosculer. » Intus erat dilectus, dum « in principio erat Verbum, » foris, dum « verbum caro factum est. » « Et deosculer, » facie ad faciem videam, et ore ad os loquar. De quo osculo in exordio libri satis arbitror dictum. « Et jam me nemo despiciat. » Ante adventum Christi intra angustias Judaeae tantum fuit Ecclesia, post 624Aascensionem in toto mundo dilatata fuit, et venerabilis effecta. Prius dictum fuerat, « Notus in Judaea Deus, » nunc autem dicitur: « Exaltare super coelos, Deus, et super omnem terram gloria tua. » Potest et aliter intelligi. Sugit ubera matris Christus, cum historiis priscae legis Christianus populus reficitur. Matrem quippe Ecclesiae, id est corpus Christi Synagoga dicunt, cui in primordio eloquia divina credita sunt, et qui in Veteri Testamento sub figura tegebatur, per Evangelium in novo in veritate reperitur.

« Apprehendam te, » id est prompta ac fideli devotione venientem accipiam. « Et ducam te in domum matris meae. » Peracta carnis dispensatione, redeuntem laetis ducam luminibus in domum matris, 624Bid est coelestis Hierusalem, quae est mater nostra. « Ibi me docebis, » id est facies me potiora sperare dona, quam in lege habuissem. « Et dabo tibi poculum ex vino condito, » id est ferventem amorem variis virtutum pigmentis odoratum vel ornatum. « Et mustum malorum granatorum meorum, » id est gloriosorum martyrum sanctorum triumphum, qui ferventissima charitate per ferrum flammasque ad te transire non dubitant. Aliter: Ducit Ecclesia Christum in domum matris coelestis Hierusalem, quando cum omnibus bonis quae per suam gratiam consecuta est, etiam ipsa perduci meretur. Ibique docetur, « ut jam non in aenigmate per specula, sed facie ad faciem videat, » quando optimos et dulcissimos protulerit martyrium fructus, velut mustum malorum 624Cgranatorum.

« Laeva ejus sub capite meo, et dextera illius amplexabitur me. » In laeva incarnationis Christi dona; in dextera, futura sanctorum cum Christo gaudia exprimuntur. Vel in laeva praesens vita, quam sancti sub mentis capite ponunt, quia despiciunt. In dextera beatitudo, quam sancti ob nimium desiderium amplexabuntur. De hoc versu superius latius abundeque disputatum est.

« Quae est ista quae ascendit de deserto deliciis affluens? » Verum et hinc jam disseruimus, sed quia repetitio versuum, firmitatis, ut saepe diximus, indicium est, nunc tangamus breviter. Vox Synagogae vel prophetae admirantis, quomodo Ecclesia de deserto gentilitatis in sponsis subito amplexus ascendisset: 624Dcui per prophetam dicitur: « Pro eo quod fuisti derelicta, et odio habita (Isa. LX), » etc. Et in psalmo: « Erraverunt in solitudine in inaquoso (Psalm. CVI). » Deliciis affluens, id est omnium donorum pulchritudine. « Innixa super dilectum meum. » Quia omnia quae habet, ad gratiam ipsius refert stabilita in eo fide, spe et charitate, quem mihi soli committendam credebant. « Suscitavi te sub arbore malo. » Respondit sponsa pro Synagoga. Ipse sponsus sub arbore crucis a perpetua morte liberavi te, ut apostolos et caeteros electos ex Judaea. « Ibi corrupta est mater tua, ibi violata est genitrix tua. » Id est, major pars plebis Christum negando et Barrabam eligendo, ad crucem reprobata est.

625A« Pone me ut signaculum super cor tuum, et sicut signaculum super brachium tuum. » Signaculum in brachio vel digito, ob memoriam cuilibet rei ligamus. Per « cor, » cogitatio, et per « brachium, » operatio designatur. Id est: Si me velis habere sponsum, intus sit charitas fide non ficta, et foris operatio devota, et sic recipies fidem meam, ut eam immaculatam custodias. Et quia per crucem meam signaculum accepisti, thesaurum justitiae quem in te contuli, a nullo fure auferri pertimescas, ut tam cogitatione quam etiam operatione ad illud signum recurras, et sicut ego te intantum dilexi, ut mori voluissem pro te, ita econtrario sancti tui mortem non recusent pro me. « Quia fortis est ut mors dilectio, dura sicut infernus aemulatio. » Fortis 625Best usque ad mortem dilectio mea in te, o Synagoga, sed tua aemulatio dura in me fuit sicut infernus: sed verte aemulationem in dilectionem, et eris mihi sponsa, et soror, et amica. « Lampades ejus, lampades ignis, atque flammarum. » Dilectionis lampades corda sunt fidelium, in quibus charitas inhabitat, semper splendida et nitore charitatis munda atque igne Spiritus sancti ardentia, flammarumque bonae operationis enitentia globo.

« Aquae multae non potuerunt exstinguere charitatem, nec flumina obruent illam. » Aquas multas et flumina, tentationum dicit incursus, quae visibiliter seu invisibiliter animas fidelium impugnant, quibus charitas non cedit. « Si dederit homo omnem substantiam domus suae pro dilectione, quasi nihil 625Cdespicient eam. » Sicut vero totius substantiae divitias pro dilectionis Christi magnitudine dantes, quasi nihil despiciebant eum, id est mundum. Alio vero modo ex superioribus pendet sententiae sensus. Cum enim dixisset, « aquae multae » vel flumina « non potuerunt exstinguere charitatem, » subjunxit: « Si dederit omnem substantiam domus suae, » etc., ut illuc intelligatur quod Apostolus ait: « Et si distribuero in cibos pauperum omnes facultates meas, et si tradidero corpus meum ita ut ardeam, charitatem autem non habeam, nihil mihi prodest (I Cor. XI). »
